GS-VLA introduces a lightweight, plug-and-play framework that uses 3D Gaussian splatting to canonicalize viewpoints for frozen Vision-Language-Action (VLA) policies, improving robustness to camera shifts without any policy retraining. It is the first approach to directly leverage 3D Gaussian-based novel-view synthesis for observation-space adaptation in VLA policies. This work addresses a critical robustness issue in VLA policies, where even small camera displacements can drastically reduce task success rates (e.g., from ~90% to ~10% on LIBERO). By providing a plug-and-play solution that avoids expensive fine-tuning and catastrophic forgetting, it enhances the practical deployment of VLA policies in real-world robotics. The framework uses a 4M-parameter 3D-Gaussian canonicalizer prepended to a frozen VLA policy, reformulating viewpoint shifts as a localized novel-view synthesis problem under a Locality assumption. Experiments show improvements across three axes: policy architectures, unseen task suites, and perturbation scales, without modifying policy weights.

## [DA-WAM: Aligning World Model Futures with Driving Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19085) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DA-WAM introduces a unified framework that integrates predictive representation learning, action-conditioned future modeling, and trajectory scoring under a single decision-making objective for autonomous driving. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on NAVSIM-v1 and NAVSIM-v2 benchmarks. This work addresses a critical gap in autonomous driving by ensuring that predicted futures are directly informative for trajectory selection, rather than being decoupled from planning. It could influence future research on world models and end-to-end driving systems, potentially improving safety and decision-making in complex scenarios. DA-WAM uses an online encoder and a momentum target to maintain predictive supervision during planner optimization, and an action-conditioned predictor generates distinct future latent states per trajectory candidate. A future-latent-conditioned factorized scorer evaluates these states, with safety-critical hard negatives providing additional supervision near planning boundaries.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: World models in autonomous driving aim to predict future scenes based on current observations and actions, helping systems anticipate changes and make safer decisions. However, traditional approaches often separate future representation learning from planning, or share predicted states across trajectory candidates, which can dilute action-specific consequences. Action-conditioned future modeling explicitly incorporates the agent's actions to compare possible outcomes, while trajectory scoring evaluates candidate trajectories to select the best one.

## [DistScan Detects Backdoors via Pre-NMS Distribution Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19088) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DistScan, a new backdoor detection framework for object detection models, was introduced. It detects backdoors by monitoring shifts in pre-NMS prediction class distributions on clean inputs, achieving a 27.32 percentage point improvement in average detection accuracy over the best baseline. This addresses a critical gap in backdoor detection for object detection, as existing methods fail to generalize to scene-level attacks. DistScan's simplicity and effectiveness could enhance security in safety-critical applications like autonomous driving and surveillance. DistScan requires no model weight access, no trigger knowledge, and no additional training. It was validated on MS-COCO and PASCAL VOC across two architectures and three scene-level attack scenarios, outperforming existing methods significantly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Object detection models are vulnerable to backdoor attacks where hidden triggers cause misbehavior. Existing detection methods rely on trigger inversion or architecture-specific assumptions, which fail on scene-level attacks where a single trigger affects all objects. DistScan exploits the observation that backdoor injection shifts the pre-NMS prediction class distribution away from training class frequencies.

## [Open-MOPD: Fixing Capability Imbalance in Multi-Teacher Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19098) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Open-MOPD, a controlled benchmark for multi-teacher on-policy distillation (M-OPD) on SmolLM3-3B-Base with oracle routing, and reveals a capability integration gap where standard M-OPD captures only 35.6% of the available headroom. The proposed framework, incorporating token-share balancing, gap-aware dynamic budget allocation, and student reward refresh, elevates headroom recovery to 83.4%. This work addresses a critical gap in multi-teacher on-policy distillation for reinforcement learning, providing a reproducible recipe and challenging the common assumption that gradient conflict is the main obstacle. The findings have practical implications for training generalist LLMs and RL agents, potentially improving efficiency and performance in multi-domain scenarios. The capability integration gap is attributed to three orthogonal factors: structural sequence-length disparities, dynamic convergence drift from non-uniform learning rates, and multi-step reward staleness from asynchronous policy updates. The Open-MOPD framework is fully open-sourced, including training trajectories and evaluation suites, on an academically accessible hardware budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-teacher on-policy distillation (M-OPD) is a post-training paradigm that consolidates domain-specialized RL experts into a single generalist student via dense token-level reward supervision. Unlike traditional knowledge distillation, M-OPD operates in policy space and uses reinforcement learning, where multiple teachers supervise student rollouts. The paper introduces a controlled benchmark with oracle routing to isolate capability integration from routing ambiguity, allowing a clear diagnosis of the optimization dynamics.

## [Experimental Astrolinguistics: LLMs and the Kangaroo Effect](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19124) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper makes astrolinguistics experimental by using two LLMs with deliberately incompatible constructed lexicons to study translation ambiguities, introducing the 'kangaroo effect' and a protocol that eliminates mistranslations. Across 400+ runs, the protocol produced no undetected mistranslations and exceeded a passive baseline's coverage. This work operationalizes Quine's indeterminacy of translation in a controlled experimental setting, providing a framework for studying communication with minds that categorize reality differently. It could inform future astrolinguistic efforts and improve machine translation systems dealing with ambiguous or novel concepts. The protocol combines cross-situational elimination, pre-registered predictive probes, active scene selection, a stricter recovery round, and quarantine. Under informant noise, zero kangaroos persist up to 2% per-word noise; at 10% noise, the protocol predominantly abstains rather than errs. Words outside the scripted hypothesis space are recovered by a generate-and-test loop where an LLM proposes rules and the script verifies them.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Astrolinguistics, the study of interstellar languages and communication with extraterrestrial intelligences, has been largely speculative since Freudenthal's Lincos in 1960. Quine's indeterminacy of translation argues that there can be multiple incompatible translation manuals that fit all evidence, leading to the 'kangaroo effect' where a word is silently attached to the wrong referent. This paper uses LLMs with constructed lexicons to simulate such scenarios and test protocols for avoiding mistranslations.

This paper introduces a novel perspective on gradient-boosted ensembles by treating leaf values as coordinates in a high-dimensional space, enabling exact contrastive explanations and a recourse method that reconstructs model decisions to 6.2e-15 precision. This work provides a theoretically grounded and exact method for contrastive explanations and recourse in gradient-boosted models, which are widely used in high-stakes tabular data applications. It addresses limitations of existing approximate methods and offers a more actionable and verifiable approach, potentially improving trust and fairness in automated decision-making. The method is evaluated on five tabular datasets with repeated cross-validation, showing Pareto-non-dominated performance on effort vs. realism for credit datasets. When restricted to actionable changes, it retains 58% validity compared to 41% for the strongest baseline, a distinction not captured by standard evaluation.

Contrastive explanations answer 'Why P rather than Q?' by identifying features that lead to different outcomes, which is crucial for recourse—providing actionable steps for individuals to change an unfavorable decision. ## [Interpretable AI Predicts 2026 Summer Dry Anomaly in Central China](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19163) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A deep learning model with layer-wise relevance propagation (LRP) interpretability predicts a summer 2026 dry anomaly in central China by translating atmospheric circulation predictions into precipitation estimates. The model consistently indicates this dry anomaly from March to May initializations, with a mechanism linked to central equatorial Pacific warming. This work demonstrates a novel application of deep learning to seasonal precipitation forecasting, providing physically interpretable explanations that enhance trust in AI-derived climate projections. It could improve regional drought preparedness and inform evidence-based decision-making before observational data confirm the anomaly. The model uses LRP to identify northerly winds as the dominant driver of the predicted dry anomaly, and perturbation tests confirm that removing these features eliminates the anomaly. Retrospective evaluations show higher predictive skill in analogue years featuring central equatorial Pacific warming persisting from winter to summer, which induces anomalous cyclonic circulation over the western North Pacific-South China Sea-South China region.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Seasonal precipitation anomalies are largely regulated by atmospheric circulation, which dynamical models predict more reliably than precipitation itself. Layer-wise relevance propagation (LRP) is an explainable AI technique that redistributes relevance scores from the output layer back to the input layer, helping to interpret deep neural network predictions. Central equatorial Pacific warming can influence East Asian monsoon systems, affecting regional precipitation patterns.

## [ADEPT: Pre-Training and Post-Training for Dexterous Manipulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19182) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ADEPT introduces a large-scale reinforcement learning framework that pre-trains dexterous policies on generic object reposing tasks and post-trains them for long-horizon tasks, enabling zero-shot sim-to-real transfer on two high-DoF robot embodiments from raw visuo-tactile perception. This work addresses a challenging problem in dexterous manipulation by providing a practical pre-training/post-training recipe that avoids learning skills from scratch for each new task. It could significantly accelerate the deployment of multi-fingered robots in real-world applications, impacting robotics and reinforcement learning research. ADEPT uses a joint-space Geometric Fabric to mediate between the RL policy and the robot, and distills post-trained teachers into perceptive students. It demonstrates zero-shot sim-to-real transfer on a 23-DoF Kuka-Allegro with two RGB cameras and a 29-DoF Flexiv-Sharpa with two RGB cameras and five vision-based tactile sensors, solving long-horizon tasks at human-level speed.

## [SPADE: Self-Play Framework Generates Adaptive Training Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19197) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SPADE introduces a self-play reinforcement learning framework where a single LLM acts as both an environment designer and a reasoning agent, generating executable training environments with an OpenAI Gym-style interface. The framework enables continuous improvement by having the agent learn in environments that are dynamically adapted to its current capabilities. This approach addresses a key limitation in current LLM training, where static environment pools keep the goal distribution fixed as the model scales. By making environment design a learnable component, SPADE could enable more efficient and open-ended self-improvement for language agents, potentially improving performance across various benchmarks. The framework uses a regret signal, estimated by the gap between the agent's reward with and without privileged hints, to guide the environment designer toward tasks at the edge of the agent's capabilities. Key components include grounding the designer on documents from a large pretraining corpus and providing an accumulated environment memory. Scaling to 30B parameters, SPADE improves over the strongest fixed-environment baseline by +5.3 on average across eight benchmarks, and by +5.7 on BFCL-v4 multi-turn and +13.9 on ACEBench-Agent for tool use.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Self-play in reinforcement learning involves agents generating their own curricula by interacting with copies or past versions of themselves, which helps create diverse and challenging scenarios. OpenAI Gym provides a standard interface for RL environments with reset() and step() functions, which SPADE adopts for its generated environments. The regret signal in RL quantifies the difference between the optimal strategy and the one employed, guiding the learning process.

## [Conformal Calibration for Safe Policy Control in RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02196) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a conformal calibration method that uses a safe reference policy to regulate new policies, providing finite-sample guarantees for user-defined risk tolerance even with non-monotonic bounded loss functions. It is applied to natural language question answering and biomolecular engineering, demonstrating safe exploration from the first deployment. This work addresses a critical challenge in high-stakes reinforcement learning: enabling safe exploration without requiring the user to specify the correct model class or tune hyperparameters. It offers a principled way to balance safety and performance, potentially improving adoption of RL in real-world applications. The method leverages conformal prediction, which assumes exchangeability of data, to calibrate the new policy's behavior against a safe reference. Unlike previous conformal methods, the theory handles non-monotonic bounded loss functions, and the paper introduces a new policy control setting. Experiments show performance improvements while maintaining safety.

**Background**: In reinforcement learning, a policy defines how an agent acts in an environment. Safe exploration is crucial in high-stakes domains where violations can cause harm. Conformal prediction is a framework for uncertainty quantification that provides distribution-free prediction sets with finite-sample guarantees, typically assuming exchangeability. This paper applies conformal calibration to regulate policy changes, ensuring safety without conservative optimization.

## [Interval POMDP Shielding Ensures Safety for Imperfect-Perception Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.20728) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a novel shielding algorithm that uses interval POMDPs to provide finite-horizon safety guarantees for autonomous systems with imperfect perception. The approach builds confidence intervals for perception outcome probabilities from finite labeled data and computes a conservative belief set to construct a runtime shield. This work addresses a critical safety issue in autonomous systems that rely on learned perception, where misclassifications can lead to unsafe actions. By providing formal safety guarantees with high probability, it enhances the reliability of AI-controlled systems and could influence safe AI deployment in real-world applications. The algorithm models the system as a finite Interval Partially Observable Markov Decision Process (POMDP) with discrete states and actions, and computes a conservative set of beliefs consistent with observations. Experiments on four case studies demonstrate that the shielding approach improves safety over state-of-the-art baselines.

**Background**: Partially Observable Markov Decision Processes (POMDPs) are a framework for decision-making under uncertainty where the agent cannot directly observe the full state. Interval arithmetic is a technique that represents values as ranges to handle measurement errors and uncertainties. Shielding is a runtime safety enforcement method that monitors and intervenes in an AI controller's actions to prevent safety violations.

## [Event-Causal RAG: A New Framework for Long Video Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06185) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Event-Causal RAG (EC-RAG), a lightweight retrieval-augmented framework that segments ultra-long videos into semantically complete events represented as State-Event-State (SES) structures, stored in dual vector-graph memory. It also presents ECV-1H, an hour-scale benchmark with over 150 hours of video and 1,251 human-annotated QA pairs, showing accuracy improvements of 4.96%–11.67% across three open-source video foundation models. This framework addresses a critical limitation of large vision-language models in handling ultra-long videos, where maintaining coherent event memory and reasoning over long-range causal relationships is challenging. By enabling efficient streaming processing and improved accuracy, EC-RAG could advance applications in video surveillance, autonomous driving, and long-form content analysis, and influence future research in video understanding and retrieval-augmented generation. EC-RAG uses a dual visual-audio sentinel mechanism to segment video streams into events, and employs bidirectional graph retrieval to recover predecessor and successor events during question answering. It runs on a single RTX 5090 GPU with 32 GB memory, continuously processing videos with controlled streaming memory usage, and supports 24-hour-plus processing with negligible incremental memory.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large vision-language models (LVLMs) have shown strong performance on short and medium-length videos but struggle with ultra-long videos due to visual-token growth and context length limits. Traditional retrieval methods that index fixed-length clips often fragment complete events, weakening state-transition modeling. Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) combines retrieval with generation to improve reasoning, and causal event modeling helps capture state transitions. The EC-RAG framework integrates these ideas by segmenting videos into causal event structures and using dual memory for efficient retrieval.

## [RULER: Representation-Level Verification for Machine Unlearning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27569) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RULER introduces a set of representation-level verification metrics (M2 and M4) for machine unlearning, revealing that models can pass output-level tests while still encoding forgotten data in intermediate representations. Empirical results show that four approximate unlearning methods pass output-level evaluation but fail representation-level checks in 10 of 12 conditions. This work addresses a critical gap in machine unlearning verification, as current output-level metrics are insufficient to guarantee true data removal. It could influence future unlearning research and has practical implications for privacy, model auditing, and regulatory compliance. The oracle-comparative metric M2 measures whether forget-set records occupy the same representational position as in a retrained model, while the oracle-free metric M4 detects residuals from the unlearned model's internal similarity structure alone. Using a linear mixed-effects model, M2 detects significant residuals (p<0.05) in 10 of 12 conditions, with effect sizes growing as the forget fraction increases; M4 also serves as a pre-unlearning diagnostic across tabular, image, clinical text, and face-identity settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Machine unlearning aims to remove the influence of specific training records from a deployed model without retraining from scratch. Current verification protocols typically rely on output-level metrics such as membership inference, retain accuracy, and forget-set accuracy, but these can be satisfied even when the model still encodes forgotten data in its intermediate representations. RULER introduces representation-level metrics to address this limitation, providing a more thorough verification approach.

## [LiteOdyssey: Teaching AI Expert Reasoning for Rare Disease Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16149) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces liteOdyssey, an agentic diagnostic system that uses Policy Iteration with Human Feedback (PIHF) to convert expert reasoning into a scalable AI capability, improving rare disease diagnosis accuracy from 26.5% to 59.3% on benchmark cases. This work demonstrates a novel method for embedding expert reasoning into LLMs without expensive model retraining, potentially making high-quality rare disease diagnosis more accessible. It also shows that such policies generalize to unseen diseases and transfer across models, which could impact medical AI deployment. The system achieved 59.3% top-1 accuracy on 1,243 benchmark cases spanning 722 rare diseases, compared to 26.5% without the policy. The policy transferred without modification across closed- and open-weight models, and in 515 Undiagnosed Diseases Network patients, blinded physicians rated its differentials more often exact and less often unhelpful.

## [ChainWorld Benchmark Reveals Computer Use Agents Struggle with Long-Horizon Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21654) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ChainWorld composes atomic OSWorld tasks into 347 long-horizon desktop workloads of length two to four, and evaluates four computer use agents under single-turn and multi-turn protocols. The maximum chain completion rate is only 31%, with multi-turn evaluation improving completion for three models but both protocols remaining challenging. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in evaluating computer use agents, which are typically tested on atomic tasks but must handle sustained multi-objective work in real-world scenarios. The low completion rates highlight the limitations of current agents and provide a new standard for future research and development. The workload is created through directional compatibility search, preserving the source evaluators. The two protocols expose different failure profiles: single-turn failures concentrate on artifact precision, while multi-turn failures often reflect session management problems such as fragmented progress and later turn disengagement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Computer use agents are AI systems that operate graphical user interfaces to perform tasks on desktops or web applications. OSWorld is a benchmark providing a public desktop environment with visual interaction, file-based state, and execution-based evaluation. ChainWorld builds on OSWorld by chaining atomic tasks into longer sequences, simulating realistic desktop work that requires maintaining state across multiple objectives.

## [Train the Model, Not the Reader: Decodability Supervision for Verifiable Activation Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20379) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper reveals that reconstruction-based explanations of LLM activations can be unfaithful, with only ~2% of specific claims being reconstruction-dependent. It introduces two audit protocols and RECAP, a co-trained auxiliary method that improves verifiability by making designated content decodable by an independent probe. This work addresses a critical flaw in interpretability evaluation, showing that high reconstruction scores do not certify individual claims. For AI safety, RECAP enables designated content to be checked against a probe rather than asserted by prose, making explanations more trustworthy and harder to game. RECAP trains linear heads alongside the target model to keep designated content decodable, at a negligible +0.001-nat cost. On RECAP-trained models, fresh verbalizers state the designated content truthfully and codes vanish; an independent probe ranks true claims above false ones (AUC 0.96 vs 0.82 without RECAP), and remains robust against adversarial edits (AUC 0.95 vs 0.51 control).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Natural Language Autoencoders (NLAs) are a recent interpretability method that converts LLM activations into natural language explanations, using an activation verbalizer (AV) and an activation reconstructor (AR). The standard evaluation measures reconstruction fidelity, but this paper shows that such scores can be gamed and do not guarantee truthfulness of individual claims. RECAP (Readable Encodings via Co-trained Auxiliary Predictors) adds linear prediction heads during training to ensure designated content is decodable by an independent probe, providing a more verifiable alternative.

## [Imperfect Value Alignment Can Lead to Catastrophic AI Outcomes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28881) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper formalizes conditions under which an AI with imperfect value alignment, defined by an eta-catastrophic value function, can cause catastrophic outcomes. It shows that overoptimizing a proxy to human values can drive expected human value below a threshold eta, even after idealized alignment training. This work provides theoretical grounding for a core AI safety concern, highlighting the dangers of overoptimization and motivating designs that limit optimization pressure, such as quantilizers. It is highly relevant to AI alignment research and could influence future safety protocols. The paper introduces the concept of an eta-catastrophic value function, which guarantees that expected human value falls below eta in the limit of optimizing power. It identifies conditions on the human value function and proxy accuracy that lead to deployment of such agents, emphasizing that pre-deployment training alone may be insufficient.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: AI alignment aims to ensure AI systems act in accordance with human values. A common fear is that optimizing for an imperfect proxy of human values can lead to catastrophic outcomes, a phenomenon known as overoptimization. This paper models the alignment problem where an agent undergoes idealized alignment training before optimizing the world, and explores conditions under which such training fails to prevent catastrophic behavior.

## [Reconstruction Benchmark Shows LLMs Struggle to Recover Research Ideas from Bibliographies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16645) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Reconstruction, a blind benchmark that tests whether language models can recover a paper's research idea from its pre-publication bibliography alone. Across 643 papers and six domains, frontier models achieved only 3-15% match rates, while a multi-agent pipeline with cross-model review and Swiss tournament selection raised this to 23-42%. This benchmark provides a rigorous, anti-leakage evaluation of LLMs' scientific reasoning abilities, highlighting significant limitations in current models. It offers a new tool for assessing AI research capabilities and could drive improvements in model design and evaluation methodologies. The benchmark uses a strict anti-leakage protocol including temporal citation cutoff, anonymous reference IDs, and frozen per-paper bibliographies. The multi-agent pipeline combines cross-model review with a Swiss tournament over aligned hypothesis slots, achieving approximately a 2.4x lift over the best single-model baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Language models are increasingly used for scientific discovery, but evaluating their ability to generate novel research ideas is challenging. Benchmarks like Reconstruction aim to provide controlled evaluations by withholding the target paper and using an independent judge to match hypotheses. The Swiss tournament system, commonly used in chess, pairs competitors with similar scores to efficiently rank them, and here it is adapted to select the best hypothesis among multiple candidates.

## [GRIP: Stochastic Bottleneck for RAG to Prevent Query Dominance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16776) ⭐️ 8.0/10

GRIP introduces a stochastic bottleneck for retrieved evidence in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), forcing the evidence channel to encode only residual information not available from the query. This approach reduces query-latent mutual information by roughly 30x (from 14.8 to 0.47 bits) and cuts hallucination by 73% across five reasoning benchmarks. This addresses a significant and under-explored failure mode in RAG systems—query dominance—where high-capacity encoders make retrieved evidence functionally irrelevant. By providing a theoretically grounded solution, GRIP could improve the reliability of RAG systems in real-world applications, reducing hallucinations and enhancing reasoning quality. GRIP imposes capacity asymmetry: the decoder retains full-dimensional access to the query, while retrieved evidence passes through a severe stochastic bottleneck. Residual-alignment analysis shows that the bottleneck output occupies subspaces less aligned with the query than baseline representations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances large language models by retrieving external documents to ground responses. However, high-capacity encoders can let the query dominate the latent state, making retrieved evidence irrelevant—a failure mode called query dominance. The information bottleneck principle, which constrains information flow to preserve relevant information, underpins GRIP's approach.

## [NINJA Jailbreak Attack Exploits Long-Context LMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.04707) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced NINJA (Needle-in-haystack jailbreak attack), a new method that jailbreaks aligned language models by appending benign, model-generated content to harmful user goals. Experiments on HarmBench show it significantly increases attack success rates across models like LLaMA, Qwen, Mistral, and Gemini. This reveals a fundamental vulnerability in long-context language models, where even benign contexts can be crafted to bypass safety alignment. The attack is low-resource, transferable, and less detectable, posing significant risks to AI safety and security. The attack's effectiveness depends on the position of harmful goals within the context, and it is compute-optimal: under a fixed compute budget, increasing context length can outperform increasing the number of trials in best-of-N jailbreak. The method is less detectable than prior jailbreaking approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Long-context language models can process up to a million tokens, enabling complex tasks like computer-use agents. However, their safety implications are not fully understood. Previous research, such as 'Lost in the Middle,' showed that models may not use long contexts effectively, which NINJA exploits by hiding harmful goals among benign content.

## [Tensor Decomposition Boosts Pilot-Limited MIMO Channel Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.04083) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes a structure-informed hybrid estimator that formulates pilot-limited MIMO channel estimation as low-rank tensor completion, comparing CP and Tucker decompositions and integrating a lightweight 3D U-Net to learn residual components. On synthetic specular channels, Tucker completion improves NMSE by 10.88 dB over least squares and 7.83 dB over orthogonal matching pursuit at 10% pilot density, while CP outperforms Tucker by 13.11 dB at SNR=20 dB. This work addresses a critical challenge in 6G MIMO systems where pilot overhead limits channel estimation accuracy. By combining tensor decomposition with deep learning, it offers a robust solution that outperforms both traditional and purely data-driven methods, potentially enabling more efficient and accurate channel estimation in future wireless networks. The hybrid estimator has two regimes on DeepMIMO channels: Tensor-NN(Tucker) remains stable at 2% pilot density where CP diverges, while a CP-guided variant becomes best from 4% pilot density, achieving -16.44 dB at 8% and -20.34 dB at 20%. Empirical analysis shows sample complexity scales with intrinsic channel dimensionality (dominant paths) rather than ambient tensor size.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: MIMO (Multiple-Input Multiple-Output) systems rely on accurate channel state information (CSI) for beamforming and data transmission, but acquiring CSI requires pilot overhead that becomes prohibitive as bandwidth scales toward 6G. Tensor decomposition, such as CP and Tucker, can exploit the low-rank structure of MIMO channels to estimate them from sparse pilot observations. The 3D U-Net is a convolutional neural network architecture originally designed for volumetric segmentation, here adapted to learn residual components beyond the low-rank structure.

## [AutoOR: RL and Synthetic Data Autoformalize OR Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16804) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AutoOR introduces a scalable synthetic data generation and reinforcement learning pipeline that trains LLMs to autoformalize optimization problems across linear, mixed-integer, and non-linear categories. Applied to an 8B model, it achieves state-of-the-art or competitive results on six established OR benchmarks, matching significantly larger frontier models. This work is significant because it addresses the bottleneck of requiring specialized OR expertise to translate natural language problem descriptions into solver-ready formulations. By enabling smaller models to achieve frontier-level performance, AutoOR could accelerate industrial decision-making and broaden access to optimization tools across manufacturing, logistics, and scheduling. AutoOR generates verified training data from standard optimization forms and uses solver execution feedback as the reward signal for RL post-training. For a non-linear problem class involving physical dynamics, where frontier models score near 0%, a curriculum RL strategy bootstraps from limited initial data to make the class tractable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Autoformalization is the task of automatically translating informal natural language descriptions into formal, machine-verifiable representations. In operations research, this means converting problem statements into solver-ready formulations (e.g., linear or mixed-integer programs). Reinforcement learning from execution feedback (RLEF) is a technique where a model is trained using feedback from running its outputs in a solver, providing a strong reward signal for correctness. Synthetic data generation helps create large-scale training datasets without manual annotation.

## [EgoMemReason: Benchmarking Memory-Driven Reasoning in Week-Long Egocentric Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09874) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EgoMemReason is a new benchmark for evaluating memory-driven reasoning in week-long egocentric videos, covering entity, event, and behavior memory types. It includes 500 questions with an average of 5.1 evidence segments and 25.9 hours of memory backtracking, and tests 17 methods, with the best achieving only 39.6% accuracy. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in long-horizon egocentric video understanding, moving beyond perception tasks to memory-driven reasoning. It highlights the limitations of current multimodal models and agentic frameworks, providing a foundation for advancing memory-aware AI systems for applications like smart glasses and embodied agents. EgoMemReason evaluates three memory types: entity memory (tracking object state changes), event memory (recalling and ordering activities), and behavior memory (abstracting recurring patterns). The benchmark includes six core challenges and reveals that performance degrades as evidence spans longer temporal horizons, indicating distinct failure modes for each memory type.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Egocentric video understanding is crucial for next-generation visual assistants like smart glasses and embodied agents, which must reason over continuous visual experiences spanning days. Existing long-video benchmarks focus on perception tasks such as moment localization or summarization, not on reasoning that integrates evidence across multiple days. Memory-driven reasoning requires models to accumulate information over time, recall prior states, track temporal order, and abstract recurring patterns, which is a fundamental challenge for current AI systems.

## [ICICLE: In-Context Indexing for Generative Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26902) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ICICLE introduces an in-context indexing framework that enables generative retrieval models to incorporate newly added documents at inference time without retraining, using a [COPY]-based routing mechanism and preference-based calibration. Experiments on MS MARCO and NQ320K show improved retrieval of new documents while preserving performance on previously seen documents. This work addresses a critical limitation of generative retrieval—the high cost of corpus expansion—by enabling incremental updates without retraining, which could make generative retrieval more practical for dynamic corpora. It also highlights source-selection calibration as a key bottleneck, guiding future research in scaling in-context retrieval. ICICLE combines a [COPY]-based routing mechanism, preference-based calibration, and large context adaptation to distinguish context-grounded retrieval from parametric retrieval. The analysis shows that high-shot degradation is mainly caused by routing failure, indicating that source-selection calibration is a key bottleneck for scaling in-context generative retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Generative retrieval (GR) maps queries directly to document identifiers (docids) using parametric knowledge, but adding new documents requires retraining, which is costly and can cause catastrophic forgetting. In-context learning allows models to perform tasks from examples without parameter updates, and this work applies that idea to retrieval by supplying new documents as inference-time evidence.

COMPACT-VA introduces a planning-aligned working memory framework using conditional VQ-VAE to compress long-context inputs in monolithic vision-action models, achieving over 6% improvement in success rates and 3.3x speedup with 2.7x memory reduction. This addresses a critical computational bottleneck in real-time autonomous driving systems, enabling efficient long-context processing without backbone modifications. ## [Density-Matrix Order Parameter Reveals Two Fine-Tuning Failure Modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07559) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a density-matrix-based order parameter to analyze language model fine-tuning, distinguishing two failure modes—kinematic and structural—where cross-entropy loss decreases but the correct token fails to outrank a near-synonym. The framework is validated across five transformer architectures and predicts a held-out architecture's critical learning rate within 2.1%. This work addresses a significant and underappreciated failure mode in language model fine-tuning, providing a novel theoretical framework that could help practitioners diagnose and mitigate fine-tuning issues. The findings may impact how the ML community understands and addresses silent failures in model optimization. The order parameter combines the predicted distribution with embedding overlap, treated as a density matrix over a non-orthogonal basis, and decomposes into a signal term and a drag term. The jumps persist under LoRA even though the token embedding matrix never changes, indicating the discontinuity lies in the softmax readout, not a geometric phase transition.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Fine-tuning is a form of transfer learning where a pre-trained model is adapted to a specific task. In language models, cross-entropy loss is commonly used, but it can decrease even when the model fails to rank the correct token above a near-synonym, a silent failure. The density matrix is a concept from quantum mechanics used to describe systems in mixed states, and here it is applied to model the distribution over a non-orthogonal basis of token embeddings.

## [Training-Time Augmentation Mitigates Overfitting in Data-Constrained LLM Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16246) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper systematically investigates training-time data augmentation as a regularizer for autoregressive language model pretraining under data-constrained conditions. It introduces three orthogonal augmentation categories—token-level noise, sequence permutations, and target offset prediction—and shows they delay overfitting and lower validation loss, with random token replacement being the most effective individual method. As AI labs approach a data ceiling, this research offers a practical solution to enable productive multi-epoch training on fixed corpora, addressing a critical challenge in LLM pretraining. The findings could help improve model quality and data efficiency across the industry, especially for organizations with limited access to fresh high-quality text. The three augmentation categories are token-level noise (masking, random replacement), sequence permutations (right-to-left prediction, Fill-in-the-Middle), and target offset prediction (predicting x_{t+i} for i > 1). Combining augmentation categories further lowers the minimum validation loss, and all code and data are available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive (AR) pretraining typically trains models to predict the next token in a sequence, but it overfits severely when data is limited and training runs for many epochs. Data augmentation techniques, such as Fill-in-the-Middle (FIM), have been used in code models to improve performance, but their systematic application to general AR pretraining under data constraints has been underexplored. This paper addresses that gap by categorizing and testing various augmentation strategies.

## [First-Token Broadcasters: Language Identity Heads in Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22361) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces Language Identity Head Ablation (LIHA), a causal intervention method that zeros individual attention heads to measure language switch rates. Applied to GPT-2, it identifies 'first-token broadcaster' heads, led by L6H1, that control language identity, and shows that instruction tuning in Qwen2.5-1.5B reorganizes these circuits toward early-layer localization. This work advances interpretability of multilingual LLMs by pinpointing specific attention heads responsible for language identity, which could inform targeted fixes for language mixing errors. The finding that instruction tuning localizes language circuits to early layers has implications for model design and fine-tuning strategies. LIHA was applied to GPT-2 with 2,700 prompt-language pairs across seven languages, identifying L6H1 with a switch rate of 0.32 (3.23 sigma above mean). Compensation after ablation is statistically significant (p < 10^-5) and follows a hierarchical pattern, recruiting only heads in layers above the ablated head. In Qwen2.5-1.5B, the base model showed near-flat influence (max SR=0.016), while the instruct model concentrated influence at layer 0, led by L0H5 (SR=0.224).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Transformers use multi-head attention, where each head can specialize in different functions. Causal ablation, such as zeroing a head, helps determine its causal role in model behavior. Multilingual models sometimes generate in the wrong language, and understanding the underlying mechanisms is key to fixing this. This paper builds on prior interpretability research that uses attention head intervention to test causal importance.

## [SLAI T-Rex: Full-Parameter Post-training of DeepSeek-V4 on Ascend SuperPOD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20145) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This report presents SLAI T-Rex, an end-to-end optimization framework for full-parameter post-training of trillion-parameter MoE models on Ascend NPU SuperPOD, achieving 34.22% MFU and a 2.93x improvement over the baseline. It also establishes CPT and SFT workflows for Operations Research tasks, producing a specialized model that outperforms GPT-5.4-Mini and the base model. This work demonstrates a viable full-stack pathway for efficient trillion-parameter model post-training on Ascend infrastructure, which is significant given the growing interest in non-GPU hardware for large-scale AI. It also shows that domain-specialized models can achieve state-of-the-art performance on complex reasoning tasks, potentially influencing future model development and deployment strategies. The framework spans model-level parallelism, computation-communication orchestration, and low-level kernel execution, addressing memory pressure, communication overhead, and inefficient kernels. The OR-oriented dataset contains 10K high-quality SFT samples across four task categories and three problem representations, and the specialized model achieves 71.81% average zero-shot Pass@1, outperforming GPT-5.4-Mini by 3.98 points and the base model by 11.27 points.

Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models activate only a subset of parameters per token, enabling trillion-parameter scale with manageable compute, but they introduce challenges in memory and communication during distributed training. ## [Approximate Speculative Decoding Boosts LLM Inference Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03447) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Approximate Speculative Decoding (ASD), a training-free verifier that relaxes strict token matching to reuse target-greedy suffixes, improving speculative decoding efficiency. Experiments show throughput gains of 3.05%–15.26% over matched strict verification across seven Qwen3-14B + DSpark-14B tasks. This method addresses a key bottleneck in speculative decoding by allowing approximate acceptance of mismatches, which can significantly enhance inference efficiency for LLMs. It requires no new draft model or fine-tuning, making it easy to adopt in existing deployment pipelines. ASD replaces binary first-mismatch truncation with budgeted longest-prefix selection, using a local target-logit regret gate, a per-block exception cap, and a persistent request-level regret budget. It exactly reduces to standard greedy verification when the budget is zero, and the source code is publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Speculative decoding is an inference-time optimization for autoregressive LLMs where a smaller draft model proposes candidate tokens and a larger target model verifies them in parallel, preserving the original output distribution while reducing latency. Standard greedy verification stops at the first mismatch, discarding the remaining target-scored suffix; ASD relaxes this to reuse contiguous target-greedy suffixes, improving efficiency without altering the draft model.

This paper introduces a novel multi-robot problem formulation for ordered storage and retrieval at maximum capacity in puzzle-based storage systems, and presents an online prioritized multi-agent path planning algorithm that guarantees complete, deadlock-free parallel execution even at full storage density. This work addresses a critical trade-off in automated warehouses between storage density and retrieval throughput, offering a scalable solution that can significantly improve efficiency in high-density storage environments. The algorithm's near-linear makespan improvement with the number of robots and robustness to departure sequence uncertainty makes it highly relevant for real-world logistics and robotics applications. The algorithm builds on prior work that constructs arrangements supporting sequential storage and retrieval without relocating loads, and exploits structural invariants to achieve scalability. Experiments show near-linear improvement in makespan with up to C robots, where C is the width of the grid's open side, and robust arrangements handle bounded uncertainty in departure sequences with negligible impact on makespan.

This paper formalizes the ordered storage and retrieval problem in PBS and proposes a prioritized planning algorithm that guarantees completeness and deadlock-free execution.

The paper introduces RUPA, a trajectory-level uncertainty quantification framework for LLM agents that models execution history as a directed graph and propagates uncertainty over it to capture long-range error accumulation. Existing UQ methods rely on local signals and fail to detect failures originating from earlier steps. RUPA addresses this gap, improving reliability and enabling earlier failure detection in complex agent deployments. RUPA is evaluated on benchmarks including τ-2, Terminal-Bench-2, and GAIA using six open-source LLMs, consistently outperforming existing UQ methods. It combines propagated uncertainty with behavioral features and goal-alignment information.

This paper introduces the first closed-loop Environment State-Text Injection (ESTI) attack on LLM-driven embodied agents, exploiting environment-state text as an independent attack surface to compromise planning and execution. The attack achieves up to 89.32% improvement in planning-level and 43.69% in execution-level attack success rates over existing baselines. This research exposes a critical security gap in embodied AI systems, where environment-state text is often trusted without verification. It highlights the need for robust defenses in real-world applications like robotics and autonomous agents, where compromised planning can lead to physical consequences. The ESTI attack reformulates adversarial objectives as false state evidence compatible with the current environment, affecting planning and execution through object properties, spatial relations, affordances, task-stage rules, and execution feedback. The authors developed ESTI-Bench to evaluate attack propagation across the planning-to-execution closed loop, testing on ProgPrompt/VirtualHome, VoxPoser/RLBench, and AI2-THOR/iTHOR.

## [Co-RL: Multi-Agent RL Enables Unsupervised Reasoning Emergence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17253) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Co-RL introduces a multi-agent reinforcement learning framework where multiple parameter-independent models are trained cooperatively using peer-derived rewards, enabling unsupervised reasoning emergence and mitigating training collapse. It consistently outperforms base models and prior label-free approaches across text-only and multimodal benchmarks. This work addresses a critical limitation in self-rewarding RL, where training on self-generated feedback can lead to homogenization and collapse. By leveraging cohort diversity, Co-RL offers a scalable path to improve reasoning in LLMs and VLMs without ground-truth labels, potentially reducing annotation costs and advancing AI alignment. Co-RL trains multiple decoupled models with no shared parameters, using rewards derived from peers. Increasing cohort diversity through heterogeneous model families, sizes, and rephrased samples reduces correlated errors; it yields average gains of 3.0-8.6% across seven text-only benchmarks and 2.3-7.2% across four multimodal benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) has been used to improve reasoning in language and vision-language models, but often relies on ground-truth supervision, which is costly and scarce. Self-rewarding RL reduces this dependence by letting models derive rewards from their own completions, but it can lead to biased feedback and training collapse. Co-RL addresses this by using a cohort of diverse models that provide rewards to each other, promoting diversity and stability.

## [MotoSafety: Edge-AI Framework for Two-Wheeler Collision Risk Under Time Pressure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17823) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced MotoSafety, a new edge-AI framework and a dataset of over 129,000 labeled multivariate time-series samples from 153 simulator rides under No, Low, and High Time Pressure scenarios. The framework achieves 94.97% accuracy and 99.33% ROC AUC, outperforming ten baselines including TimesNet and LLM4TS, with only 1.15M parameters and 0.135 ms latency. This work addresses a critical safety gap for powered two-wheeler riders in low- and middle-income countries, where cognitive stressors like time pressure are understudied. The lightweight, edge-deployable model can enable real-time collision risk assessment on low-cost hardware, potentially improving road safety and supporting the Safe System Approach in intelligent transportation systems. The dataset captures 64 attributes per sequence, including vehicle motion, rider control actions, spatial proximity, and rule compliance indicators. Using ground truth time pressure as an inductive bias improves accuracy from 94.09% to 94.97%, and using only 21 IMU+GPS features still achieves 93.91% accuracy, indicating practical deployment feasibility.

**Background**: Powered two-wheeler riders face high collision risks, especially in low- and middle-income countries, but research on cognitive stressors like time pressure is limited. Edge-AI refers to running artificial intelligence models on local devices (e.g., CPUs) rather than cloud servers, enabling low-latency, privacy-preserving applications. The Learned Temporal Importance (LTI) concept is a deep learning principle that learns which time steps are most important for prediction, improving efficiency and accuracy.

## [Self-Evolving Agents as Dynamic Graph Transformation: A New Survey Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18104) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This survey proposes a novel framework that models LLM-based agent evolution as dynamic graph transformation, where agent state is represented as a dynamic graph with typed nodes, edges, and subgraphs updated through schema-constrained rewrites. It organizes existing methods into four taxonomies: node/feature evolution, edge/topology evolution, subgraph activation, and cross-component co-evolution. This perspective bridges two previously separate research lines—graph-based agent surveys and self-evolving agent surveys—offering a unified structural lens for designing and governing self-evolving agents. It could influence future research by highlighting dynamic graph learning as reusable infrastructure for agent evolution, potentially impacting AI/ML and systems research communities. The survey maps nine dynamic-graph-learning subfields to agent-evolution capabilities and discusses their adaptations and possible failure modes. It also proposes five types of graph-aware evaluation and governance protocols from a dynamic-graph perspective, complementing end-task evaluation.

This survey connects these lines by framing agent evolution as dynamic graph transformation, where agent state is modeled as a dynamic graph.

This paper benchmarks several LLMs, including Mistral, LLaMA, Falcon, and multilingual BERT, for hate speech detection in Roman Urdu, comparing zero-shot inference with LoRA-based parameter-efficient fine-tuning on the PURUTT dataset with over 72,000 annotated comments. The results show that LoRA fine-tuning significantly improves performance, achieving F1 scores above 0.93 compared to 0.56 for zero-shot. This work addresses the underexplored problem of hate speech detection in low-resource languages like Roman Urdu, which is widely used on social media in South Asia. Demonstrating that parameter-efficient fine-tuning can achieve high performance with limited computational resources is valuable for deploying NLP systems in resource-constrained settings and for moderating online content in such languages. The study uses the PURUTT dataset, which contains parallel Urdu and Roman Urdu toxic comments, and evaluates models on zero-shot inference and LoRA fine-tuning. The findings highlight that updating only a small fraction of trainable parameters via LoRA yields substantial performance gains, making it a computationally efficient approach for low-resource language tasks.

## [RDFdL: Bridging RDF Knowledge Graphs and Differential Dynamic Logic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18165) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces RDFdL, a framework that integrates RDF with Differential Dynamic Logic (dL) to represent and reason about both static knowledge and continuous dynamics of physical systems. It provides a syntactic representation of differential equations and state space ranges in RDF and SHACL, with semantics defined via translation to dL, and enables verification results to be queried through SPARQL. This integration bridges the gap between static knowledge representation and dynamic system verification, which is crucial for AI-driven cyber-physical systems. It allows domain experts to leverage existing RDF knowledge graphs and SPARQL queries to access safety and reachability verification results, potentially improving the accessibility and usability of formal verification in CPS. The framework uses Apache Jena for ontology-driven RDF reasoning and KeYmaera X as the theorem prover for dL. The paper sketches an application in manufacturing, demonstrating the practical potential of the approach.

**Background**: RDF (Resource Description Framework) is a standard for representing knowledge graphs, but it lacks the ability to model dynamic behaviors described by differential equations. Differential Dynamic Logic (dL) is a logic for specifying and verifying hybrid systems that combine discrete and continuous dynamics. SHACL is a W3C standard for validating RDF graphs, and Apache Jena is a Java framework for building Semantic Web applications. KeYmaera X is a theorem prover for dL.

## [Jaccard Distance Triangle Inequality Generalized to Arbitrary Lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18194) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that the Jaccard distance satisfies the triangle inequality on arbitrary lattices when the valuation is strictly positive, monotone, and modular, generalizing previous results that required distributivity. It also establishes conditions for relatively complemented distributive lattices and identifies supermodularity as a necessary condition for the standard generalized Jaccard distance to be a metric. This generalization removes the restrictive assumptions of Boolean algebras, making the Jaccard distance applicable to broader structures like quantum logic and open-ended data streams. It has practical implications for metric learning, formal concept analysis, and machine learning, where such distances are widely used. The paper also adapts the symmetric-difference Jaccard formulation for submodular valuations to sectionally complemented distributive lattices. It proves that supermodularity is a strict requirement for the standard generalized Jaccard distance to be a valid metric, and discusses applications in quantum information theory and open mathematical problems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: The Jaccard distance measures dissimilarity between sets by the proportion of unique elements relative to their union. Traditionally, its generalization to lattices relied on Boolean algebra properties like distributivity and global bounds, which limit applicability. Lattices are algebraic structures with partial orders, and valuations are functions that assign real numbers to elements, with properties like modularity and supermodularity influencing metric behavior.

## [ComponentBench: Diagnosing Component-Level Failures in Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18307) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ComponentBench introduces a benchmark and diagnostic pipeline for component-level evaluation of computer-use agents, featuring 2,910 programmatically verified tasks across 97 canonical UI components and cleaned human reference trajectories. It evaluates seven models across four observation and action spaces, revealing that design choices can shift task success by over 30% for the same model. This benchmark fills a critical gap between long-horizon workflow benchmarks and atomic GUI-grounding tests, enabling more precise diagnosis of component-level failures in computer-use agents. It provides a scalable pipeline for auditing structural difficulty and synthesizing failure analyses, which could significantly improve agent evaluation and development across the AI/ML community. The benchmark includes 97 canonical UI components instantiated as 2,910 tasks across widely used component libraries, paired with human reference trajectories for evaluating both task success and interaction efficiency. Notably, GPT-5 mini's success drops from 83.1% with accessibility-tree observations to 48.9% with coordinate-only Pixel control, and even the fastest configuration takes 3.7x longer than the human reference.

ComponentBench addresses this by providing a library-agnostic ontology and a diagnostic pipeline to systematically evaluate and improve agent performance on modern web UIs.

## [ICCM: Detecting Structural Ambiguity for Safe Wearable Stress Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18397) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the Individual Conformal Coupling Monitor (ICCM), a lightweight pre-inference monitor that quantifies subject-specific coupling divergence and routes each window to classify, defer, or abstain without retraining the downstream classifier. It is validated on WESAD and Stress-Predict datasets, showing negative correlations between ambiguity and accuracy. This work addresses a critical safety issue in wearable stress classification where models can achieve high average accuracy but fail for specific individuals. ICCM provides an interpretable signal to flag unsupported physiology, potentially improving personalized health monitoring and enabling safer deployment of such systems. On WESAD, a Random Forest achieves 93.0% mean accuracy but yields F1=0 for Subject 14, illustrating structural ambiguity. ICCM changes false-positive counts from 29 to 27 and 94 to 92, though not significant, and withholds 3 of Subject 14's stress windows but does not fully repair the missed-stress failure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Wearable stress classification uses physiological signals like ECG, EDA, and respiration to detect stress. Conformal prediction is a framework for uncertainty quantification that provides statistically valid prediction sets. Structural ambiguity refers to situations where individually plausible signals form an inter-signal pattern poorly supported by a person's non-stress reference, leading to individual-level failures despite good average performance.

## [SDDL Framework Boosts Small LLM Scheduling Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18409) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SDDL, a neuro-symbolic framework that translates natural-language scheduling problems into compact, solver-aligned representations, improving feasibility for resource-constrained language models. On a 300-instance benchmark, SDDL boosts verified feasibility from 23.7% to 55.3% for the strongest configuration, with a 0.0% median optimality gap. This work addresses a critical limitation of small language models in combinatorial optimization, enabling them to approach the performance of much larger frontier models in resource-constrained settings. It has practical implications for real-world scheduling applications where deploying large models is impractical. SDDL delegates low-level modeling and search to a deterministic compiler and external solver, expressing problem structure rather than generating solutions or solver code. The two strongest SDDL configurations reach 55.3% and 28.3% feasibility, up from direct-generation baselines of 23.7% and 1.3%, and solver-code baselines of 21.7% and 7.0%.

## [UMER: Pair-Aware Discriminative Reasoning for Universal Multimodal Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18504) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UMER proposes a unified multimodal embedding and ranking framework that replaces item-wise Chain-of-Thought reasoning with Pair-Aware Discriminative Reasoning, jointly learning contrastive embeddings and discriminative ranking within a single MLLM. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on the MMEB-V2 benchmark under comparable experimental settings. This work addresses a key limitation in existing CoT-based multimodal retrieval methods, which lack explicit evidence to distinguish positives from hard negatives. By unifying embedding and ranking, UMER could improve retrieval accuracy for diverse instruction-aware tasks, benefiting applications like search engines and recommendation systems. UMER introduces a Pair-Aware Discriminative CoT paradigm that takes positive and hard-negative query-candidate pairs as reasoning inputs, encouraging explicit comparison of matching and discrepancy evidence. It also employs a complementary mutual distillation strategy to transfer reliable pairwise preferences between the embedding and ranking functions, and supports budget-adjustable inference.

**Background**: Universal multimodal retrieval aims to support diverse instruction-aware retrieval tasks by mapping different modalities into a shared semantic space. Recent MLLM-based embedding methods derive representations from hidden states, and Chain-of-Thought reasoning has emerged as a strategy to enhance embeddings by encoding intermediate semantic evidence. However, existing CoT methods typically reason over items in isolation, lacking explicit pairwise comparison to handle semantically confusable hard negatives.

## [HN-CLIP: Adaptive Similarity Margins for Dense-Caption Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18521) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces HN-CLIP, a method that uses the text encoder's own text-text geometry to construct per-negative adaptive similarity margins, mitigating premature saturation in contrastive learning for dense-caption retrieval. It improves R@1 by +2.4 to +4.3 over strong baselines and trains 2.4x faster than GOAL and 5.4x faster than StructXLIP. This work addresses a critical yet overlooked issue in dense-caption retrieval: premature saturation of the InfoNCE loss under strong pre-trained initialization. By providing a simple, efficient solution that improves performance across multiple frameworks, it could influence future contrastive learning designs in multimodal retrieval and beyond. HN-CLIP adds a detached caption-similarity matrix to the negative logits, assigning larger margins to more similar captions without mining, synthesizing, or resampling negatives. It requires only one caption-similarity matrix and a masked logit addition during training, with no auxiliary data, additional parameters, offline preprocessing, or inference-time overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Dense-caption retrieval involves matching images with detailed textual descriptions that capture rich visual elements. Contrastive learning, particularly the InfoNCE loss, is commonly used to align image and text representations, but it can suffer from premature saturation when many near-duplicate captions exist, leaving hard negatives unresolved. Adaptive margin contrastive learning addresses this by introducing instance- or context-aware margins, often computed from geometry or difficulty measures.

## [Pairwise Ranking Beats Single-Action RL for Offline Explanation Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18531) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes a cost-efficient framework for offline explanation selection in LLM-based recommender systems, separating generation from selection. It shows that pairwise ranking (LambdaRank) outperforms single-action RL methods (PPO, GRPO, DPO) on both Google Local and MovieLens-1M datasets. This finding offers a practical, low-cost solution for industrial explainable recommendation systems, reducing serving costs by avoiding per-request LLM generation. It also provides a reliable ordering of selectors, guiding practitioners to choose pairwise ranking over more complex RL approaches. The framework uses a frozen candidate pool of explanations generated ahead of time, with a small CPU-resident selector that runs in under 100 ms. LambdaRank achieved F1=0.500 on Google Local and F1=0.329 on MovieLens-1M, with seed variance below 0.003 F1. The KG-path selectors achieved USR=1.000 on Google Local, but target a different objective.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based recommender systems generate explanations for recommendations, but doing so per request is costly. This paper separates generation from selection: explanations are pre-generated into a candidate pool, and a lightweight selector picks one at request time. Pairwise ranking methods like LambdaRank learn to order items by comparing pairs, while single-action RL methods like PPO, GRPO, and DPO use one labeled candidate per rollout.

This paper presents a production-deployed framework that uses AI-powered Product Research Agents to bridge search and CRM workflows, identifying users with exploratory purchase intent and low engagement, and delivering personalized recommendations via WhatsApp. In a 23-day deployment with about 15,000 WhatsApp notifications, the framework achieved substantial CTR improvements over traditional campaigns. This work demonstrates a practical, scalable solution to a common e-commerce challenge: proactively re-engaging customers who leave for external research. By integrating search, recommendation, and CRM systems, it shows how AI agents can drive measurable business outcomes like increased CTR, downstream purchases, and GMV impact. The framework uses grounded multi-agent product research, combining behavioral signals, external knowledge, and enterprise catalog data. The deployment focused on mobile product discovery, and secondary engagement was observed through message forwarding and sharing, indicating broader customer reach.

## [FACET Framework Synthesizes Executable Terminal Tasks with State Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18580) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FACET (Fine-grained Agentic Construction of Executable Tasks) is a new framework that synthesizes complex terminal tasks by preserving source intent and ensuring cross-artifact consistency. It reconstructs agent skills into coherent scenarios, repairs the execution environment, and uses the container state as shared grounding for instructions, solutions, and verifiers. This addresses a critical bottleneck in training terminal agents: the lack of scalable, high-quality executable supervision. By generating tasks with dense executable checks and demonstrating performance improvements on Terminal-Bench 2.1, FACET could accelerate progress in AI agent development and software engineering automation. FACET uses execution-based validation and targeted repair to correct artifact-specific failures without regenerating valid components. Fine-tuning models across multiple scales consistently improves performance on Terminal-Bench 2.1, and analyses support the importance of environment-grounded construction for task validity and solution-verifier alignment.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Terminal agents are AI systems that interact with command-line interfaces to perform tasks. Training them requires executable supervision, which includes an instruction, an initialized environment, a reference solution, and an executable verifier. Synthesizing these tasks is challenging because inconsistencies between artifacts can make tasks unsolvable or incorrectly evaluated. FACET addresses this by grounding all artifacts in a shared, repaired environment state.

## [Lightweight Model Predicts LLM Reasoning to Cut Costs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18591) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces BudgetDoc, the first multimodal benchmark for model-budget-performance trade-offs in document tasks, and DRB, a ~1B-parameter estimator that predicts LLM reasoning performance to enable dynamic budget allocation. DRB achieves a 0.753 weighted F1 and matches or improves accuracy in 9 of 15 configurations while reducing cost. This work addresses the practical challenge of expensive and inefficient uniform reasoning budget allocation in LLMs, offering a cost-effective solution that maintains accuracy. It could significantly impact efficient LLM inference, especially for document-centric tasks where visual complexity varies. DRB uses SigLIP-2 and Qwen3-0.6B as components, predicting a 7-class ordinal performance label for any (document, prompt, model, budget) configuration. The study evaluates five frontier models across three datasets, and preliminary results suggest potential for cross-model generalization.

Document tasks add complexity because visual layout affects reasoning difficulty. A pre-flight estimator can predict performance before expensive inference, enabling dynamic budget allocation to optimize cost and accuracy.

## [LLMs Fail to Distinguish Determined from Undetermined Preference Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18631) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper formalizes two types of indeterminacy in preference reasoning—epistemic and structural—and demonstrates through experiments that state-of-the-art LLMs systematically fail to distinguish determined from undetermined instances, even in verification settings. This work highlights a critical limitation in LLM-based decision-making, which is essential for alignment and collective intelligence. It suggests that current models may be unreliable in real-world preference reasoning tasks where information is incomplete or no valid solution exists. The paper introduces a hierarchy of tasks to evaluate LLMs' ability to handle indeterminacy. The results show miscalibrated reasoning, meaning models are overconfident or underconfident in their judgments about whether a solution exists.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Preference reasoning is fundamental to AI alignment and collective decision-making. In real-world scenarios, preferences are often incomplete or contradictory, leading to indeterminacy where no clear solution exists. Social choice theory provides concepts like Condorcet's paradox that illustrate structural indeterminacy, while epistemic indeterminacy arises from incomplete information.

## [Competence, Not Accuracy: A Diagnostic for Reference-Free Judge Gates in Skill Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18719) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper formalizes reference-free LLM judges as latent solvers, deriving a closed-form bound on their discriminability (ROC-AUC) in terms of judge competence and answer-space size, along with a necessary condition for usefulness in skill optimization. It also introduces a non-intervening probe to measure judge scores on real optimization runs without altering decisions. This work provides a theoretical foundation for using LLM judges as validation gates in text-space skill optimization, potentially enabling automated skill learning in tasks without verifiable rewards. The proposed diagnostic offers a cheap pre-deployment check to predict whether a judge gate will be effective, which could impact the broader LLM evaluation and reinforcement learning communities. The model yields a necessary condition c > 1/k for discriminability, where c is judge competence and k is answer-space size, and shows that marginal AUC is confounded by item difficulty while a within-question estimator is not. The closed-loop study demonstrates that the screen predicts which type of gating error occurs, and benchmark accuracy overstates the competence that matters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Text-space skill optimization evolves a natural-language skill document for a frozen agent, accepting candidates through a validation gate that typically relies on verifiable rewards. Replacing the verifier with an LLM judge could lift this restriction, but whether such a gate carries usable signal is untested. This paper addresses the prior question of whether a judge's scores can separate correct from incorrect answers at all, formalizing the judge as a latent solver whose evaluation capacity is bounded by its solving capacity.

## [PWAL: Neuro-Symbolic Enthymeme Completion via Possible-World Marginalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18820) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces PWAL (Possible-World Atom-Link Formalization), a neuro-symbolic method that extends a prior pipeline from missing-premise to missing-claim selection and replaces binary entailment with logical-resistance scores. PWAL uses weighted Partial MaxSAT and marginalizes logical resistance over alternative semantic-link configurations, improving strict accuracy by 2.95-30.86 percentage points over Top-Link on five tasks. This work addresses a gap in enthymeme completion by providing a transparent, logic-based method that handles uncertainty in semantic links, which is crucial for argumentation and natural language inference. The significant accuracy gains and reduced tie rates suggest PWAL could improve downstream tasks like automated reasoning and debate analysis. PWAL keeps translated formulae fixed and marginalizes logical resistance over alternative cross-formula semantic-link configurations, recording translated formulae, sampled link configurations, and resistance components for every comparison. Evaluated on ARCT, CDED, iDebate, AAE2, and alphaNLI, it also reduces tie rates by 4.57-58.00 percentage points, with accuracy still increasing by 0.45-6.04 points when ties receive half credit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: An enthymeme is an argument with a hidden premise or conclusion, common in rhetoric and everyday reasoning. Neuro-symbolic methods combine neural learning with symbolic reasoning, and weighted Partial MaxSAT is an optimization technique that maximizes the weight of satisfied clauses while allowing some to be violated. This paper builds on these concepts to handle uncertainty in semantic links between logical formulae.
