## [Verifiable Abstention Framework Boosts AI Leak Localization Accountability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18836) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a verifiable abstention framework for AI-based leak localization in water distribution networks, where an executor agent falsifies hypotheses against a digital twin and an LLM-audited supervisor certifies or abstains from actions. Under field-grade noise, the approach improves decision precision from a 32% forced baseline to 96% on acted events. This work addresses the accountability gap that prevents utilities from trusting AI localizers, offering a defensible route to autonomous water-infrastructure operation. It demonstrates a novel combination of physics-based digital twins with LLM auditing, which could influence broader AI decision-making under uncertainty. The framework uses a physics-grounded executor agent to falsify hypotheses (leak, demand, sensor, valve) against a digital twin, while an independent supervisor agent with an LLM auditor checks evidence against a code-verifiable contract. On an independent benchmark, it acts on only 4 of 33 leaks, all correct, and a 194-event register yields five excavation dispatches, three correct, with 44% survey recovery at full district precision.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Water utilities lose a substantial share of treated water to leakage, but rarely trust AI localizers because they cannot prove when they should not act. Digital twins are virtual replicas of physical water systems that enable real-time monitoring and simulation. Verifiable abstention is a decision-making approach where a model can abstain from acting when uncertain, with the abstention being auditable and verifiable, often using LLMs to check compliance with contracts.

## [EvoResearcher: Training-Free Self-Reflection with Cost-Bounded Early Stopping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18884) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EvoResearcher introduces a training-free, inference-time self-reflection protocol that enables a frozen LLM to iteratively generate, self-critique, and revise answers until a CONFIRMED sentinel triggers early stopping. It is validated on Big-Bench Hard, GSM8K, and MATH, achieving 82-88% early termination at equal accuracy on clean BBH. This approach offers a practical alternative to expensive reinforcement learning fine-tuning (e.g., GRPO) for improving reasoning in LLMs, making it accessible to more researchers and applications. By enabling cost-bounded self-verification, it could reduce inference costs while maintaining accuracy, benefiting deployment in resource-constrained environments. The protocol uses four self-reflective meta-reward components—correctness, efficiency, reflection depth, and tool-call diversity—implemented as prompt-level mechanisms with zero gradient updates. Cross-model replication was performed on Qwen2.5-72B, and the tool-call diversity component is validated only in prompt-level form; environment-level and multi-agent extensions remain future work.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning post-training methods like GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) have been used to enhance reasoning in LLMs, but they require expensive training and a controllable environment. Inference-time techniques aim to improve performance without additional training by leveraging the model's own capabilities during generation. Self-reflection involves the model critiquing and revising its own outputs, which can be guided by meta-rewards to balance correctness and efficiency.

## [TestifAI: Tomography-Based Robustness Testing for Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18900) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TestifAI is a new deep learning testing framework that efficiently estimates model robustness against combinations of semantic perturbations with discrete severity levels. It introduces partial model tomography to reconstruct model behavior in a multi-perturbation space from lower-order projections, reducing inference costs by 60-80% while maintaining high accuracy. This framework addresses a critical gap in systematic exploration of perturbation combinations for deep learning systems, which is essential for safety-critical applications like autonomous driving. By enabling efficient and accurate robustness estimation, TestifAI can help improve AI safety and reliability in real-world deployments. TestifAI trains an auxiliary model on test results from up to two perturbations to predict outcomes for three or four perturbations, achieving an aggregate robustness estimation error of less than 7%. Experiments were conducted on five image and language classification tasks, demonstrating the framework's effectiveness across different domains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Deep learning models are increasingly deployed in safety-critical domains, necessitating thorough testing to ensure correct behavior. Robustness testing typically involves thousands of inferences to verify output stability under bounded input perturbations, but existing frameworks lack systematic exploration of combinatorial perturbation spaces. TestifAI builds on concepts from combinatorial testing and model tomography to address this challenge.

## [Adaptive Memory and Reflection Multi-Agent System for Medical QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19029) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the AMR agentic system, a multi-agent framework for medical question answering that uses specialized agents with dedicated memory and reflection-based feedback to improve reasoning. It demonstrates strong performance on MedQA and MedMCQA benchmarks, with ablation studies showing that combining agent-specific memory, reflection, and external retrieval yields the best results. This work addresses key limitations of existing medical QA systems, such as lack of adaptability and persistent memory, by introducing a structured multi-agent approach. It highlights the potential of memory and feedback mechanisms for developing more trustworthy medical AI agents, which could improve clinical decision support and patient care. The AMR system routes questions through solo, collaborative, or escalated workflows based on complexity assessment, and includes consensus and ethical overseer modules for reasoning consolidation and output review. The source code is publicly available on GitHub, and the system was evaluated on MedQA and MedMCQA, which are standard benchmarks for medical question answering.

MedQA and MedMCQA are large-scale multiple-choice datasets derived from medical licensing exams, commonly used to benchmark medical QA systems.

## [FD-KVC: Ownership-Aware KV-Cache Management Boosts Dialog Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18098) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Fractional Decay KV-Cache (FD-KVC), a novel algorithm that uses a dual-channel scoring mechanism—combining cumulative attention and recency-weighted relevance—to manage KV-cache eviction in transformer-based dialog systems. It claims to outperform the H2O baseline by +6.7% on composite late-turn alignment and adapt to new topics 3.6X faster. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in efficient transformer inference for multi-turn dialog systems, where stale cache entries can degrade relevancy. By improving KV-cache management, FD-KVC could enable more responsive and context-aware conversational AI, potentially benefiting applications like chatbots and virtual assistants. FD-KVC operates entirely on CPU with negligible overhead, and its adaptive learning rate is driven by an ownership loss function to ensure convergence. The algorithm was evaluated on five multi-turn dialog scenarios with 600 dialogs each, showing improvements of +127% on topic-shift, +87% on gradual evolution, and +30% on mixed-topic dialogs, with a topic diversity of 80.6%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Key-value (KV) caching is essential for efficient autoregressive inference in transformer-based dialog systems, but existing strategies treat all cached entries uniformly or use coarse eviction heuristics that fail to adapt as topics evolve. H2O (Heavy-Hitter Oracle) is a state-of-the-art method that identifies important tokens based on cumulative attention, but it can become stale when dialog topics shift. FD-KVC introduces a recency-weighted channel to complement the cumulative attention channel, allowing the cache to preserve historically important tokens while rapidly adapting to new topics.

## [Temporal Multi-Signal Fusion Boosts Token-Level Hallucination Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18115) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a temporal multi-signal fusion method using a BiGRU for token-level hallucination detection, achieving an AUC of 0.840 on RAGTruth, an 11-point gain over an independent logistic-regression baseline. The method treats hallucination as a temporally extended span and uses sequence labeling over a 33-dimensional feature stream. This work addresses a critical limitation of existing token-level hallucination detectors, which fail when the generating model is confidently wrong. By leveraging temporal structure and multi-signal fusion, it improves detection accuracy and works on closed-source models, enhancing LLM reliability in real-world applications. The method fuses text statistics, NLI entailment, and language model surprisal into a 33-dimensional feature stream, with no access to model internals. A controlled decomposition attributes most of the gain to temporal order rather than model capacity, and the same 0.845 AUC ceiling recurs across recurrent, state-space (Mamba), and attention architectures, indicating the bottleneck lies in the feature set.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Token-level hallucination detection aims to identify which tokens in generated text are factually incorrect or unsupported. Traditional methods score each token independently from a single signal, ignoring the temporal context. This paper introduces a sequence labeling approach that uses a BiGRU to model dependencies across tokens, improving detection by leveraging the fact that hallucinations often occur in spans.

The Global Index on Responsible AI (GIRAI) has released the methodology for its 2nd Edition, introducing a refined five-dimension framework with 38 indicators to assess responsible AI governance. This edition restructures the previous three dimensions into five thematic areas and strengthens the distinction between policy existence and implementation. This methodology provides a standardized, evidence-based tool for comparing responsible AI governance across countries, which is crucial for policymakers, civil society, and AI developers to identify gaps and enforce protections. By refining the framework, GIRAI enhances its ability to influence global AI policy and accountability. The framework comprises three pillars: AI Policy (17 indicators, primary data), CSO Engagement (5 indicators, primary data), and Enabling Conditions (15 indicators, secondary data), plus a separate Use of Unacceptable Risk AI (URAI) indicator as an accountability penalty. Data is collected from 135 country-level researchers, normalized to a scale of 100, with pillar weights of 60%, 10%, and 30% respectively.

## [LLMs Underuse Deontic Modals Compared to Humans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18144) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study on arXiv (2608.18144) finds that large language models consistently underuse positive deontic modal auxiliaries (must, should, have to, had to) compared to contemporary humans, across multiple corpora and replications. The AI-generated text aligns more with formal published English than with informal digital usage. This finding highlights a gap between AI and human communication styles, which could affect the naturalness and persuasiveness of AI-generated text in contexts like customer service, education, and creative writing. It also suggests that LLMs may not fully capture the interpersonal nuances of obligation and stance in contemporary language. The study used three primary corpora, an external benchmark, two controlled replications, and an eleven-model naturalistic replication. The gap is concentrated in constructions like 'should', 'have to', and 'had to', while AI matches or exceeds humans on 'need to' in instructional and question-answering contexts, but not in persuasive student writing, indicating genre-conditional usage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Deontic modality is a linguistic modality that indicates how the world ought to be according to norms, expectations, or speaker desires, often expressed through modal auxiliaries like 'must', 'should', and 'have to'. Corpus analysis in NLP involves studying large collections of text to uncover patterns of language use, which is essential for understanding how LLMs generate language compared to humans.

## [TokenPowerSandbox: Evidence-Gated CPU-First Screening for Energy-Aware LLM Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18149) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TokenPowerSandbox introduces an evidence-gated workflow that combines an interpretable CPU-resident projector, short target-GPU probes, full-workload verification, and tamper-evident freeze-before-measurement provenance. On an NVIDIA H100 80GB serving Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct with vLLM, it achieves energy MAPE of 6.23% and 7.35% with Spearman rank correlations of 0.976 and 0.933, while safely abstaining when confidence is low. This work addresses the practical problem of costly GPU profiling for energy-aware LLM serving by offering a CPU-first screening method that reduces profiling overhead while maintaining high accuracy. The evidence-gated abstention mechanism is a thoughtful contribution that prevents overconfident predictions outside the measured scope, which is crucial for reliable deployment. The workflow uses three anchor repeats and six development workloads to calibrate workload transfer, and evaluates on a blind holdout and a separately predeclared no-refit confirmation totaling 51 post-freeze runs. A predeclared TTFT gate passes at concurrency four (9.27% MAPE) but triggers abstention below four (64.80%), demonstrating that energy accuracy cannot certify latency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Energy-aware LLM serving requires comparing configurations under realistic request shapes, but exhaustive target-GPU profiling is costly. A cheap predictor can be dangerously confident outside its measured scope, so an evidence-gated approach that combines CPU-based projection with short GPU probes and verification is proposed. The method ensures reliability through freeze-before-measurement provenance, which prevents tampering with the model before evaluation.

## [LLMs as Data Quality Annotators: When Do They Actually Help?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18158) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study evaluates LLMs on entity matching and brand mislabeling tasks, finding that a rule-based baseline matches LLM zero-shot performance on entity matching (F1=0.950 vs 0.948), while LLMs outperform on brand mislabeling (F1=0.833 vs 0.721). The study also reveals that few-shot prompt evaluation on small samples can be misleading, as a prompt revision that looked effective on a small validation set reduced full-scale F1 to 0.914. This research provides critical empirical evidence for practitioners deciding whether to use LLMs for data quality tasks, showing that simple baselines can match LLM performance when lexical signals are strong, but LLMs offer clear advantages when background knowledge is required. It also highlights the risk of over-relying on small-sample prompt evaluations, which is relevant to the broader LLM evaluation community. The study used the Abt-Buy benchmark (2,194 labeled pairs) for entity matching and 500 Amazon product listings with synthetically injected errors for brand mislabeling. Consistency testing across 200 pairs with 5 runs at temperature 0.7 showed 99.7% self-agreement, and majority voting improved F1 by only 0.005 at 5 times the inference cost.

**Background**: Entity matching is the task of identifying records that refer to the same real-world entity, often used in data cleaning and integration. LLMs are increasingly used for such data quality tasks, but their reliability compared to traditional rule-based methods is not well understood. Few-shot prompting involves providing examples in the prompt to guide the model, but its effectiveness can vary with sample size.

## [Emoji-Augmented Prompts Expose Safety Evaluation Gaps in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18164) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper tests 50 emoji-augmented prompts on four open-source LLMs (Mistral 7B, Qwen 2 7B, Gemma 2 9B, Llama 3 8B) and finds significant variation in safety robustness. Gemma 2 9B and Mistral 7B show a 10% success rate for unsafe responses, Llama 3 8B shows 6%, while Qwen 2 7B shows complete resistance (0%). This finding highlights that current safety evaluations, which rely on standard text prompts, may underestimate model vulnerabilities. It underscores the need for more comprehensive safety testing that includes alternative input representations like emojis, which could be exploited in real-world attacks. The study used a chi-square test (χ² = 32.94, p < 0.001) to confirm that the differences in outcome distributions across models are statistically significant. The scope is limited to four models and 50 prompts, so the findings are preliminary and may not generalize to other models or prompt types.

**Background**: Safety evaluations of large language models (LLMs) typically use text-based adversarial prompts to test for harmful outputs. ## [Betting Bounds Efficiently Re-certify Organ Recall in CT Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18193) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper compares distribution-free risk control methods for per-organ recall guarantees in multi-organ CT segmentation under clinical domain shift, showing that the Waudby-Smith-Ramdas (WSR) betting bound re-certifies six Tier-1 organs with only 25 local cases, versus 30-40 for Hoeffding-Bentkus (HB). This work addresses a critical challenge in deploying medical AI across institutions: maintaining per-organ recall guarantees under domain shift. The finding that betting bounds require fewer local calibration cases could reduce the cost and effort of re-certifying segmentation models in new clinical settings, improving patient safety and trust. The study uses an AMOS-trained nnU-Net, audits transfer to RAOS, and estimates local re-certification cost using case-level voxel false-negative rate (FNR). It finds that RCPS provides high-probability control of population-mean risk, while CRC gives weaker expectation control, and both require exchangeability; fixed and global thresholds offer no per-organ guarantee.

**Background**: Conformal prediction is a distribution-free framework for uncertainty quantification that produces statistically valid prediction sets under exchangeability. Risk-controlling prediction sets (RCPS) and conformal risk control (CRC) are extensions that control risk, while betting bounds like WSR provide tighter confidence sequences for sequential testing. nnU-Net is a self-configuring deep learning segmentation framework widely used in medical imaging.

## [FedCoRe: Target-Adaptive Completion for Missing Modalities in Healthcare Federated Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18311) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FedCoRe introduces a federated learning method that learns representation-level corrections to handle missing modalities in healthcare data, recovering nearly half of the performance lost when ECG is absent. This work addresses a practical and underexplored problem in federated learning for healthcare: missing modalities across clients. The proposed method shows significant performance recovery (49% of lost AUROC) on a MIMIC-derived task, potentially improving the robustness of multimodal models in real-world hospital settings. FedCoRe learns representation- or logit-space corrections rather than generating synthetic ECGs or CXR images. When a client observes a modality that may be missing at deployment, it evaluates the same example with and without that modality to obtain paired supervision; only clients with such pairs update the completion module, and validation may retain the unchanged prediction.

FedCoRe is tested on a MIMIC-derived respiratory deterioration task with simulated FL clients, where hiding ECG reduced AUROC by about 0.085.

## [OTTER: Unifying Inference and Test-Time Adaptation for VLMs via Optimal Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18339) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces OTTER, a principled test-time adaptation method for vision-language models that formulates zero-shot classification as a Wasserstein optimal transport problem to generate robust pseudo-labels, and uses a soft-label InfoNCE loss for adaptation, theoretically unifying inference and adaptation objectives. This work addresses the critical issue of noisy pseudo-labels in test-time adaptation under distribution shifts, which can mislead adaptation and degrade performance. By unifying inference and adaptation through optimal transport, OTTER improves robustness and efficiency, potentially advancing real-world deployment of VLMs. OTTER outperforms existing methods by up to 7% with state-of-the-art efficiency. The method theoretically shows that the InfoNCE loss can be reformulated as a Wasserstein OT formulation, enabling a unified framework for inference and adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) like CLIP have strong zero-shot capabilities but are sensitive to distribution shifts during inference. Test-time adaptation (TTA) aims to adapt models to unlabeled test data without labels, but existing methods often rely on noisy pseudo-labels from raw embedding similarities, which are unreliable under shift. <a id="item-152"></a>
## [Candidate-Free Control Reveals When Test-Time Aggregation Helps or Hurts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18379) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a candidate-free control for test-time aggregation, isolating the effect of candidate context under the same output-token allowance. Across AIME-2025 and HMMT-2025 with Qwen3-4B, it shows that conditioning on candidates improves accuracy when multiple are correct (+0.290) but lowers it when all are wrong (-0.123). This work clarifies the conditions under which test-time aggregation adds value beyond simply generating more tokens, which is crucial for efficient LLM reasoning. It challenges the assumption that conditioning on candidates is always beneficial, providing evidence that it can be detrimental when all candidates are wrong. The study uses a counterfactual control that allows a fresh solve under the same maximum output-token allowance, stratifying by the number of correct candidates. The c2+ and c0 conclusions survive a conservative correction for the adaptive two-benchmark procedure, but the one-correct regime remains unresolved. The evidence is limited to Qwen3-4B, two math benchmarks, and first-answer-truncated candidate fragments.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time aggregation is a technique that generates multiple reasoning traces and combines them into a final answer, often used to improve LLM reasoning performance without updating model parameters. However, it is unclear whether the benefit comes from selecting correct candidates, recombining partial solutions, or simply from the extra generation pass. This paper introduces a candidate-free control to disentangle these effects, providing a more rigorous evaluation of when aggregation is actually helpful.

## [TTSD-FAR: Test-Time Self-Distillation with Fisher-Anchored Restoration for Missing-Modality Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18386) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces TTSD-FAR, a parameter-efficient test-time adaptation framework for large video-language models (LVLMs) that combines self-distillation with Fisher-anchored restoration to handle missing modalities in emotion recognition. Experiments on MELD, DFEW, and BAH under 0%-50% missing modalities show consistent performance over long adaptation horizons, outperforming entropy-based adaptation, RAG, and perplexity-based generation. This work addresses a critical real-world challenge: missing or noisy modalities during deployment of multimodal emotion recognition systems. By enabling robust adaptation without ground-truth labels, it advances the practicality of LVLMs in affective computing and other multimodal applications, potentially improving reliability in dynamic environments. The framework uses a frozen teacher trained on complete modalities to guide an adaptive low-rank student via self-distillation, updating only a negligible number of parameters. Fisher-Anchored Restoration (FAR) monitors Fisher information stability to detect convergence versus drift and restores the student toward the teacher's anchor when distributional shifts are identified, preventing cumulative error.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal emotion recognition (ER) requires joint understanding of facial expressions, vocalizations, language, biosignals, and gestures. Large video-language models (LVLMs) have shown promise, but real-world deployment often faces missing or noisy modalities, which can be viewed as a distribution shift. Existing test-time adaptation (TTA) methods like entropy minimization or perplexity reduction do not transfer well to autoregressive LVLMs, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) degrades when the observed modality is weak. Self-distillation and Fisher information are established techniques; this work combines them in a novel way for TTA.

A new preprint (arXiv:2608.18419) applies mechanistic interpretability to LLaMA 3.1 8B, showing that the model computes and stores first differences in its internal representations and retrieves them via an induction-like circuit to solve a structure-aware numerical reasoning task. This work provides one of the first mechanistic explanations of how LLMs perform numerical sequence reasoning, offering insights that could improve model interpretability and safety. The researchers designed a task where random numbers are repeated with an offset, forcing the model to rely on structural cues. They used probing experiments and activation patching to show that LLaMA 3.1 8B computes first differences without explicit supervision and retrieves them via a mechanism similar to an induction circuit.

Researchers propose a fine-tuned Mistral-7B-instruct model as an automated 'Supervisor-in-the-Loop' system for mental health, using a tri-stream analysis on 106 DAIC-WOZ sessions. This addresses the 'supervision gap' in mental healthcare, where novice therapists lack timely senior oversight, potentially improving patient safety and training efficiency. The framework performs tri-stream analysis: therapeutic alliance tracking, latent risk prediction, and supervisory triage using a Dynamic Clinical Urgency Index (D-CUI). It achieves alliance assessment MAE of 0.105, therapeutic fidelity alpha of 0.423, and mean D-CUI of 0.370, with training converging in 105 steps on a single Tesla T4 GPU.

## [ERASE: Overlapping Forward and Backward Passes Boosts Recommendation Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18469) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ERASE introduces a scheduling method that overlaps forward and backward passes by detaching blocks' outputs, enabling early backward computation on separate CUDA streams. On a large-scale click-through-rate model, it improves training throughput by up to 9.51% while keeping normalized entropy close to baseline. This technique addresses the underutilization of modern accelerators during training of recommendation systems, potentially reducing training time and cost. It offers a practical optimization that can be integrated into existing training pipelines without major architectural changes. The method reinterprets the detachment mechanism of the Forward-Forward algorithm as a scheduling primitive, launching backward passes early on separate CUDA streams. The paper notes that a kernel saturating the device leaves no capacity for concurrency, indicating a limitation when compute is fully utilized.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional training schedules forward and backward passes as disjoint phases, leaving spare GPU capacity unused. CUDA streams allow operations in different streams to overlap, enabling concurrent execution. The Forward-Forward algorithm replaces backpropagation with local objectives, and its detachment mechanism can be repurposed for scheduling.

## [SparsePR: Training-Free Sparse Attention Boosts Video Generation and World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18484) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SparsePR, a training-free block-sparse attention method that combines Response-Coupled Partitioning with Probe-Fitted Residual Reconstruction. It achieves 1.48x-2.61x end-to-end speedups across four video generation and world models while preserving generation quality at 22.0-26.0% realized executed-pair density. This work addresses a practical bottleneck in video transformers by enabling efficient sparse attention without any training, which could significantly reduce computational costs for video generation and world model applications. It offers a theoretically grounded approach that consistently reduces attention-reconstruction error, potentially influencing future efficient attention designs. SparsePR uses sampled-query key responses to form paired K/V groups, whose centroids induce query-response coordinates for shared routing. A small set of exact query rows calibrates a call-specific affine correction from the sparse output within the output subspace observed in the probe residuals; ablations show probe fitting accounts for most of the reduction.

**Background**: Block-sparse attention is a technique to reduce computational costs in transformers by only computing attention on selected blocks of the attention matrix. Video generation and world models often rely on transformers, which are computationally heavy due to full attention. SparsePR builds on this idea by addressing the challenge of partitioning queries and keys effectively and reconstructing the residual error from skipped interactions, all without retraining the model.

## [Physics-Unrolled Neural Operator Predicts High-Fidelity Radio Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18495) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces PU-HNO, a three-stage physics-unrolled hybrid neural operator that predicts high-fidelity indoor radio maps from low-fidelity ray-tracing outputs and scene priors. It progressively captures reflection, diffraction, and scattering effects, and includes a theoretical guarantee that under conditionally unbiased label noise, the model can outperform its training labels. This work addresses a practical challenge in wireless communications by enabling high-fidelity radio map prediction without expensive simulations, which is crucial for access-point placement, coverage planning, and localization. It demonstrates that physics-informed neural operators can outperform generic image-to-image models and monolithic neural operators, potentially improving efficiency and accuracy in wireless network design. PU-HNO is a three-stage cascade that explicitly models reflection, diffraction, and scattering, rather than treating radio maps as generic images. Experiments across diverse floorplans show it outperforms image-to-image baselines, wireless learning models, and monolithic neural operators on both image-quality and wireless deployment metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Radio maps are essential for wireless decision-making tasks such as access-point placement, coverage planning, and localization, but their fine spatial details are governed by complex propagation effects and are costly to simulate accurately. Machine learning offers a path to high-fidelity radio-map prediction without running expensive high-fidelity simulations for every scene, but generating high-quality training labels at scale is difficult because affordable labels from finite-ray simulations carry residual Monte Carlo noise. Physics-unrolled neural operators, which unroll iterative optimization algorithms with physics-based consistency, have shown success in other domains like MRI reconstruction, and this work applies a similar concept to radio map prediction.

## [GCNO: Physics-Based Neural Operator for Wireless Channel Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18522) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Gramian Chebyshev Neural Operator (GCNO), a physics-based, variable-rate compressor that identifies dominant propagation paths in wireless channels, enabling more efficient feedback. It achieves better reconstruction accuracy at the same payload or lower payload at the same accuracy compared to neural feedback baselines, and transfers to unseen antenna counts without retraining. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in 5G/6G systems: the high cost of channel feedback in large antenna arrays. By providing a physics-based, variable-rate compression method that adapts to channel complexity and antenna configurations, it could significantly reduce feedback overhead and improve system efficiency, with potential impact on future wireless standards. GCNO uses receive-transmit channel structure to locate paths, a first-order Taylor correction to refine directions between grid points, and least squares to recover complex path strengths. It is trained without path labels, and the base station reconstructs the channel analytically from transmitted path tuples rather than through a learned decoder.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: In massive MIMO systems, the receiver must feed back a large complex-valued channel matrix to the base station, which is expensive. Traditional neural compressors treat this matrix like an image and use fixed-length codes, which do not adapt to channel complexity and require retraining for different antenna counts. Neural operators, such as the Fourier Neural Operator, learn mappings between functions, which can be more flexible and physics-aware.

DART-SD introduces a novel framework that models multi-turn tool-calling tasks as an Interaction-State Transition Graph (ISTG) and performs topology-guided localized correction via Critical Topological Breakpoint (CTB) detection and progressive self-distillation, avoiding full-trajectory imitation. This addresses the topological collapse problem in multi-turn tool-calling agents, which can improve policy diversity and performance on complex tasks. It offers a new paradigm for training LLM agents, potentially impacting autonomous agent development. The framework identifies the Critical Topological Breakpoint (CTB) during rollouts and retrieves success-supported recovery references, applying loss only on generated recovery steps while protecting the valid reasoning prefix. Experiments on complex multi-turn tool-calling benchmarks show significant improvements over full-trajectory baselines.

DART-SD models this as a diamond topology and uses self-distillation to guide localized corrections.

This paper proposes a novel MLaaS Performance Drift Detection (MPDD) framework for IoT environments, which uses an extraction model to learn service behavior and an Adaptive-Temporal mechanism (APDDM) to adjust monitoring frequency. Experiments show up to 22-25% accuracy improvement over baselines and a 9% reduction in miss detection rate. The framework first employs an MLaaS extraction model to identify prediction-influenced features from input-output pairs. The MPDD model then jointly captures variations in input data and MLaaS behavior, while APDDM dynamically adjusts monitoring frequency. Experiments on real-world datasets demonstrate MPDD achieves 22-25% accuracy improvement, and APDDM provides about 4% accuracy gain and reduces miss detection by 9% compared to fixed-interval monitoring.

MR-IQA-2 introduces an actor-editor-judge framework that decouples credit assignment for reasoning and rating in multimodal LLMs, using judge feedback to supervise reasoning and human ratings for rating prediction. This approach provides verifiable supervision for faithful image quality reasoning. The framework operationalizes reasoning-editing-reflection: an actor generates quality reasoning, an editor revises the image based on identified quality factors, and a frozen judge compares original and edited images to provide reflective supervision. ## [VAKE: Two-Stage RL Framework for Verifiable Activation of Parametric Knowledge in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18581) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VAKE, a two-stage reinforcement-learning framework that separates knowledge elicitation from reasoning in LLMs. It uses explicit priming to insert bridging triples as verifiable evidence, then transfers this capability to implicit reasoning, improving factual QA across seven benchmarks. VAKE addresses a key bottleneck in factual QA by enabling verifiable activation of latent parametric knowledge, distinguishing it from input-context copying. This could influence future research on knowledge elicitation and verifiability in LLMs, especially for models ranging from 3B to 14B parameters. The framework consists of a Priming stage, where a policy explicitly inserts bridging triples with rewards from a frozen model, and a Reasoning stage that tests transfer to implicit reasoning. Experiments show over 80% of inserted triples provide factual bridging knowledge not derivable from retrieved context, and more than half elicit knowledge inaccessible via direct prompting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) store factual knowledge in their parameters, but recalling and verifying this knowledge is challenging. Existing end-to-end methods entangle knowledge elicitation with reasoning, making it hard to attribute correct answers to parametric knowledge. VAKE separates these processes, using explicit priming to externalize knowledge and implicit reasoning to test transfer, building on concepts like parametric knowledge and implicit reasoning in LLMs.

## [OmniHandwritingOCR: A New Benchmark for Handwritten OCR in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18586) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OmniHandwritingOCR is a newly introduced diagnostic benchmark for evaluating multimodal large language models (MLLMs) on handwritten text and mathematical expression recognition. It comprises 77.57K labeled images across six subtasks and twelve subsets, including a difficulty-stratified multi-line formula corpus, and evaluates thirteen open- and closed-source systems using five complementary metrics. This benchmark addresses an underexplored area in OCR evaluation, as existing benchmarks focus mainly on printed text or clean single-line inputs. It provides a challenging testbed that can drive improvements in MLLMs for realistic handwritten OCR scenarios, which are crucial for document processing and knowledge extraction pipelines. The benchmark includes handwritten text recognition and handwritten mathematical expression recognition, with a key component being a difficulty-stratified multi-line formula corpus to test robustness under increasing structural complexity. Results show that current systems perform poorly on complex multi-line formulas, model rankings vary across language and formula settings, and some generative models hallucinate plausible but visually unsupported corrections.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) are increasingly used as OCR systems in document processing pipelines, but their ability to read real handwriting remains underexplored. Handwritten mathematical expression recognition (HMER) is particularly challenging due to ambiguity, variety of writing styles, and two-dimensional structure. Existing OCR benchmarks often overlook these realistic scenarios, limiting evaluation of MLLMs' language, content, structural, and visual-grounding capabilities.

## [Change Point-Aware Evaluation and Re-Calibration of PPG-Based Blood Pressure Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18639) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a fluctuation-aware evaluation framework for PPG-based blood pressure estimation using change point detection, and reveals that state-of-the-art models degrade during rapid BP fluctuations. It also proposes a targeted re-calibration framework triggered by detected change points to improve robustness. This work addresses a critical gap in PPG-based BP estimation by shifting from aggregated metrics to fluctuation-aware evaluation, which is more clinically relevant. It highlights the insufficiency of periodic calibration and offers a solution that could improve real-world continuous BP monitoring. The framework uses change point detection to identify abrupt distributional shifts in BP trajectories, avoiding heuristic thresholds like ΔBP > 10 mmHg. The proposed re-calibration is triggered by detected change points and does not require modifying model architectures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Photoplethysmography (PPG) is a non-invasive optical technique for measuring blood volume changes, and it has been explored for continuous blood pressure (BP) estimation as an alternative to cuff-based methods. Existing studies typically evaluate models using aggregated metrics like mean absolute error (MAE) over entire intervals, which can mask performance failures during rapid BP changes. Change point detection is a statistical technique used to identify times when the probabilistic structure of a time series changes abruptly, which is applied here to focus evaluation on fluctuation periods.

## [OP-UTVR: New Framework and Benchmark for Service Robotics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18672) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the orienteering problem with uncertain time-varying rewards (OP-UTVR), a new variant of the classic orienteering problem. It proposes three planners with theoretical performance bounds and a mobile service robot benchmark for evaluation. This work addresses a realistic gap in orienteering problems by handling uncertain and time-varying rewards, which is common in service robotics and delivery scenarios. It provides theoretical guarantees and a benchmark, potentially advancing practical routing and planning applications. The three planners differ in planning horizon and online adaptivity, and the paper derives theoretical bounds on their performance under reward stochasticity. Experiments in indoor environments with pedestrians show trade-offs between planning horizon and adaptivity, highlighting the effectiveness of long-horizon planning with online adaptation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: The orienteering problem (OP) is a routing problem where the goal is to select and visit locations to maximize total reward within a time budget. Traditional OP assumes rewards are known in advance, but real-world applications often have rewards that change over time and are uncertain. This paper relaxes that assumption, allowing agents to estimate and forecast rewards from observations. The proposed benchmark involves a mobile service robot navigating among pedestrians, reflecting everyday service robotics scenarios.

## [Temporal Decomposition for Historical Image Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18694) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Temporally Decomposable Image Representations (TDIR), a novel representation learning algorithm that decomposes historical photographs into separate date and content components via orthogonal subspaces. It provides theoretical proofs for the conditions of such decomposition and demonstrates its effectiveness on composed image retrieval tasks. This work bridges computer vision and temporal modeling, offering an interpretable way to navigate photographic archives while maintaining competitive retrieval performance. It could enable more intuitive search interfaces for historical datasets and inspire further research on decomposable embeddings. TDIR defines and proves conditions for temporal decomposition, characterizes the error when conditions are partially met, and shows that orthogonality emerges naturally from joint optimization without explicit constraints. It also enables transitive operations, allowing temporal information from one image to be injected into another without label supervision.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Image retrieval typically relies on high-dimensional embeddings that capture both content and metadata, but these spaces are often chaotic and hard to interpret. Temporal information is usually treated as a separate attribute rather than being integrated into the representation. This paper explores constraining embeddings to preserve temporal structure while retaining expressive power, using orthogonal subspaces to separate date and content. The approach is validated on historical photographs, where queries specify both object content and a target time period.

## [MemFuse: Multi-Source Memory Fusion Benchmark and System for Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18704) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced MemFuseBench, a benchmark for multi-source memory fusion, and MemFuse, a structured memory system that integrates fragmented observations while preserving source provenance. MemFuseBench uses a Scene-to-Sensor pipeline to generate source-tagged observations, evidence-grounded questions, and adversarial distractors. This work addresses a realistic gap in agent memory research, as existing systems and benchmarks mostly focus on single-source textual histories. By enabling systematic evaluation of temporal reasoning and cross-source evidence fusion, it could improve long-term interaction systems and embodied AI applications. MemFuse organizes memory into event-layer atomic memory and cluster-layer fused memory within a causal fusion graph, maintaining traceability to original source events during retrieval. Experiments on MemFuseBench show that MemFuse achieves the best overall performance among evaluated memory systems under all three LLM settings, especially on cross-source fusion questions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Long-term memory is crucial for agents operating over extended interactions, but most memory systems and benchmarks rely on single-source textual histories. In real-world settings, relevant information is often scattered across multiple applications, devices, users, and time, requiring agents to fuse dispersed observations into coherent episodic memories while preserving source provenance. MemFuseBench's Scene-to-Sensor pipeline synthesizes controllable scenarios into source-tagged observations, evidence-grounded questions, and adversarial distractors, enabling systematic evaluation of temporal reasoning, cross-source evidence fusion, and robustness to noise.

## [CutMix Boosts Reliability in Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18715) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study systematically evaluates CutMix data augmentation on semantic segmentation, finding it consistently improves calibration and uncertainty quality, especially under distribution shifts, with only minor impact on accuracy. This matters because reliable confidence estimates are critical for safety-critical applications like autonomous driving, where overconfident wrong predictions can be dangerous. The findings suggest CutMix can enhance model trustworthiness without sacrificing accuracy, guiding future augmentation choices. The study evaluates two representative architectures: CNN-based DeepLabV3+ and transformer-based SegFormer, across in-domain and out-of-domain scenarios. Results show CutMix primarily improves reliability rather than raw segmentation performance, a distinction crucial for deployment.

**Background**: Semantic segmentation assigns a class label to each pixel, and is used in autonomous driving and medical imaging. CutMix is a data augmentation technique that mixes images by cutting and pasting patches, commonly used to improve generalization. Calibration refers to how well predicted probabilities match actual outcomes, and uncertainty quality measures how reliable confidence estimates are.

## [Study: 10 Cases Enough for MedSAM3 LoRA Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18731) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint empirically evaluates LoRA fine-tuning of MedSAM3 for abdominal organ segmentation, showing that just 10 annotated cases achieve performance competitive with specialist systems trained on far more data. The study covers five organs in CT and MRI, validated on the AMOS22 dataset. This finding could significantly reduce the annotation burden in medical imaging, accelerating clinical adoption of foundation models. It also highlights the potential of parameter-efficient fine-tuning for specialized tasks where large annotated datasets are scarce. With 10 cases, the model achieved Dice scores of 0.68 (CT) and 0.59 (MRI) for gallbladder segmentation, where existing tools like TotalSegmentator fail (Dice 0.0004). Training took only 3–5 hours per organ on a single GPU, about 2–3 times faster than nnU-Net.

## [Flama: Unified Python Framework for APIs, ML, and LLM Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Flama, an open-source Python framework built on ASGI, has been introduced for developing and deploying production-ready APIs, machine learning services, and LLM applications. It features a type-driven, async-first design with seven subsystems, including a Rust-accelerated core and multi-backend LLM serving. Flama unifies REST API, ML, and LLM serving in a single framework, potentially simplifying the development and deployment workflow for Python developers. Its support for multiple LLM backends and wire protocols could make it a versatile tool in the growing AI service ecosystem. Flama includes a component-based dependency injection system, a pluggable schema layer (Pydantic, Marshmallow, Typesystem), automatic CRUD generation from SQLAlchemy tables, a portable .flm binary format for model packaging, and a Rust-accelerated core via Maturin. It also provides JWT authentication, WebSocket support, and OpenAPI 3.2.0 generation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: ASGI is the Asynchronous Server Gateway Interface for Python, enabling async web frameworks. Flama builds on this to offer a unified architecture for web APIs, ML model serving, and LLM inference, addressing the need for a single tool in production environments. The framework's design patterns, such as Repository and Unit of Work, are common in domain-driven design.

## [Generative AI Encodes Dominant Epistemology, Causing 'Epistemic Subordination'](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18758) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.18758) introduces the concept of 'epistemic subordination,' arguing that generative AI embeds the dominant culture's epistemology as the default infrastructure of knowledge, absorbing minority epistemologies into training data while structurally subordinating them in outputs. The paper contends this is a systemic architectural harm that existing legal frameworks fail to address because they regulate downstream decisions rather than the training process. This framework reframes AI bias as an architectural issue rather than discrete outputs, with implications across anti-discrimination law, cultural and linguistic rights, and democratic viewpoint pluralism. It suggests that legal remedies must target model training itself, potentially reshaping how AI governance is approached globally. The paper unifies three legal domains—anti-discrimination law, cultural and linguistic rights, and democratic viewpoint pluralism—showing each fails for the same structural reason: they regulate downstream decisions. The proposed remedy is to govern at the level of model training, matching the site of harm.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI models are trained on vast datasets that statistically reflect the dominant culture's languages, assumptions, and frameworks. This creates a 'statistical knowledge system' where one epistemology is the default and others are deviations. The concept of epistemic subordination builds on prior work on epistemic injustice and AI as epistemic infrastructure, which examines how AI systems shape what counts as credible knowledge.

## [SIDScope: Diagnostic Resource for Semantic-ID Interfaces in Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18779) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SIDScope is a new diagnostic resource that evaluates semantic-ID interfaces in generative recommenders, revealing that interface health is multi-signal and mechanism-conditional. It analyzes nine tokenizer exports from seven families on Amazon and Yelp data, finding that prefix alignment tracks candidate exposure only when retrieval uses SID prefixes, and that target paths can survive without unique retrieval by 1.2-3.0 percentage points. This matters because semantic-ID mappings are widely used but rarely validated, leading to potential failures in generative recommendation systems. SIDScope provides a systematic way to assess interface readiness, risks, and revalidation needs, which can improve the reliability and reproducibility of such systems. SIDScope includes frozen evidence summaries, conformance reports, trace labels, table builders, and CPU-only verifiers. It also establishes that repairing a mapping does not restore an inherited generator, requiring a separate handoff check for model reuse.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Generative recommendation (GR) uses semantic IDs (SIDs) to convert continuous semantic representations into discrete ID sequences, enabling models to generate item recommendations. SIDs are created by item tokenizers, and the mapping between items and SIDs is a reusable interface. However, these mappings often lack documentation about their coherence, structure, and resolution behavior, which can lead to issues when reused or refreshed.

This paper presents a layer-wise encoder analysis of Whisper models after multilingual medical fine-tuning, comparing zero-shot, English-only, German-only, two-stage, and direct EN+DE training across model sizes. It reveals that the optimal model varies by adaptation setting, with Whisper-Medium achieving the lowest English WER (7.72%) and combined EN+DE WER (26.30%), while German-only Whisper-Large-v3 yields the lowest German WER (44.96%) but only as a within-corpus diagnostic. This research provides deeper insights into how medical and multilingual fine-tuning reshapes internal representations of large pretrained ASR models, moving beyond surface-level WER metrics. It can guide practitioners in selecting appropriate adaptation strategies for clinical speech recognition, potentially improving multilingual medical ASR deployment. The layer-wise analysis of the two-stage Whisper-Small trajectory shows that English medical fine-tuning causes the dominant encoder shift, while multilingual continuation largely preserves the adapted representation space. Domain and language information remain highly recoverable across layers, but linearly recoverable error-predictive cues weaken as WER improves.

## [MLREF: Module-Level Reward Evolution Framework for RL via LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18827) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MLREF introduces a module-level reward evolution framework that maintains a persistent module pool of reusable reward components, evolving the pool across iterations to improve stability and reuse. Experiments on 17 tasks show MLREF outperforms strong baselines by 25.2% in locomotion and 6.6% in manipulation. This framework addresses a key bottleneck in LLM-based reward design by enabling reliable reuse of effective components, which could lead to more stable and efficient RL training. It has potential impact on the integration of LLMs with reinforcement learning, benefiting applications in robotics and autonomous systems. MLREF integrates three mechanisms: reflection-based refinement, hybrid credit assignment, and a merge strategy with rollback. The reward functions are constructed as linear combinations of modules drawn from the pool, and the pool evolves by accumulating successful modules and refining underperforming ones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Reward function design is a critical bottleneck in reinforcement learning. Large language models (LLMs) have been used to automate reward generation, but existing methods treat reward functions as monolithic programs, making it hard to preserve and reuse effective components. MLREF introduces a module pool to address this, drawing on evolutionary algorithms and LLM-based code generation.

## [Top-K Prompting Boosts Chemistry-Aware LLMs for Retrosynthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18940) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Top-K prompting, a training and inference paradigm that better captures diverse plausible reaction predictions, and compiles CREED-CCV-2+USPTO-XL, an ultra-large-scale dataset of ~45.6 million verified reactions, to train C3LM, a Chemistry Constraint-Consistent Language Model. The model achieves state-of-the-art performance on the OOD URSA-expert-2026 benchmark. This work addresses the one-to-many nature of retrosynthesis, which is often poorly captured by single-answer benchmarks. By demonstrating that LLMs and conventional models explore complementary reaction spaces, it motivates ensemble-based retrosynthesis systems, potentially improving computer-aided synthesis planning in drug discovery and organic chemistry. The model integrates fine-tuning with ChemCensor-based and novelty-oriented rewards, and the paper analyzes reaction uniqueness to show complementary reaction spaces. The URSA-expert-2026 benchmark is an out-of-distribution set designed for realistic medicinal chemistry cases, and the results highlight the benefits of ensemble approaches.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Single-step retrosynthesis is a key component of computer-aided synthesis planning, where the goal is to predict possible reactants for a given target molecule. Traditional evaluation often uses single-answer accuracy, but the one-to-many nature of the problem requires metrics that consider chemical plausibility, such as ChemCensor. Top-K sampling is a technique in language models that limits generation to the K most probable tokens, which can be adapted to produce diverse plausible predictions.

<a id="item-180"></a>
## [rEDMRec Distills LLM Reasoning into Editable Memory for Efficient Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18952) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces rEDMRec, a novel architecture that distills a teacher LLM's reasoning into four editable experience channels—long-term preference, short-term context, item-perception, and counterfactual hard-negative comparisons—managed by an LLM memory controller. A lightweight student LLM then ranks candidates by retrieving from this memory, eliminating the need to invoke the teacher during inference. This approach decouples online inference cost from reasoning depth, making LLM-powered recommendations more efficient and adaptable. It outperforms zero-shot, few-shot, RAG, and GraphRAG baselines across multiple datasets, potentially enabling practical deployment of reasoning-based recommender systems. Experiments on ML-1M, Amazon Beauty, and Steam with ten student backbones show HR@1 improvements up to 13.3% over the second-best baseline on ML-1M. Channel ablations reveal that short-term context is the only consistently helpful channel, while debate-based memory optimization reduces bank duplication by 7.4 percentage points and boosts HR@1 by up to +0.029.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models can improve recommendation by reasoning over user history, but this reasoning is expensive to repeat and typically discarded after each request. rEDMRec addresses this by compressing reasoning into a structured, editable memory that a lightweight model can retrieve from, similar to how memory systems in chatbots like ChatGPT store context for reuse. The approach builds on knowledge distillation and memory-augmented generation, aiming to make LLM reasoning reusable and cost-effective.

## [DeepWeaver: Bridging Evidence Synthesis Gap in Open-Ended QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18988) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DeepWeaver, a novel framework introduced in a recent arXiv preprint, uses Thought Block Chains (TBCs) to structure retrieved evidence into comprehensive, well-cited answers for open-ended questions. It also introduces LoQA, a high-density benchmark for evidence synthesis, and demonstrates improvements in content sufficiency, citation grounding, and detail preservation across multiple LLMs. This work addresses a critical gap in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) pipelines, where LLMs often fail to synthesize noisy evidence effectively. By improving evidence synthesis, DeepWeaver could enhance the reliability and depth of AI-powered research tools, benefiting researchers and analysts who rely on automated question answering. DeepWeaver maintains Thought Block Chains (TBCs) that group claims, salient information, keywords, and supporting evidence, using subordinate TBCs to inspect residual evidence and commit revisions. The framework is evaluated on open-ended QA over knowledge bases and the web, with code available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieve-then-generate pipelines are common for deep-research answers, but retrieval alone is insufficient; LLMs must synthesize noisy evidence into coherent answers. This process, called evidence synthesis, often fails with direct generation, leading to underused evidence, misaligned citations, and shallow summaries. DeepWeaver introduces a structured representation to bridge this gap.

## [GrabVG: Graph-Attentive Binding for UAV Visual Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18996) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GrabVG introduces a two-stage visual grounding framework for UAV imagery, decomposing the task into preattentive hypothesis search and graph-attentive feature binding. It achieves 67.31% and 80.34% Acc@0.5 on AerialVG and AerialSense, outperforming baselines by 10.55 and 8.76 percentage points. This work addresses a critical challenge in UAV visual grounding: handling small, densely packed, and visually similar objects. By improving accuracy while maintaining speed, GrabVG could enhance real-world applications like search-and-rescue, surveillance, and autonomous navigation. The framework uses distillation-guided proposal induction and text-aware hypothesis filtering to generate reliable object hypotheses, then organizes them into a sparse graph. Graph attention propagates language-guided intra-instance visual cues and inter-instance topological relationships for efficient spatial reasoning.

GrabVG draws inspiration from human visual search, which involves a preattentive stage for feature extraction and a focused attention stage for binding features.

## [Classifier-Free Visual Counterfactual Explanations via Contrastive Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19032) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a classifier-free method for generating visual counterfactual explanations by disentangling and swapping salient generative factors between class datasets, using StyleGAN2's feature space F. The approach is evaluated on three medical imaging datasets, showing superior counterfactual generation quality compared to existing methods. This work addresses limitations of existing classifier-dependent VCE methods, which are susceptible to classifier biases and failure modes. By operating directly on data distributions, the proposed method provides model-agnostic explanations, potentially improving interpretability and robustness in AI, especially in critical domains like medical imaging. The method uses the feature space F of StyleGAN2 instead of the usual W-space to improve detail preservation. Unlike conventional contrastive analysis approaches that assume salient factors in only one dataset, this framework allows multiple salient factors in each dataset, with adapted loss functions for VCE.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Visual Counterfactual Explanations (VCEs) explain image classifiers by generating minimally edited, realistic versions of an input image that change the classifier's prediction. Contrastive Analysis (CA) is a technique that disentangles common and salient generative factors between two datasets, which this paper adapts for classifier-free VCE generation.

## [ReWEIGH: Training-Free Decoding to Cut LVLM Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19075) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ReWEIGH is a training-free decoding intervention that calibrates token-level ordinal visual evidence to mitigate hallucinations in large vision-language models (LVLMs). It aggregates vocabulary ranks across visual positions and compares each candidate with a token-specific reference estimated from unlabeled images, applying a bounded penalty to under-supported tokens. Hallucinations in LVLMs undermine trust and reliability in applications like image captioning and visual question answering. ReWEIGH offers a practical, training-free solution that reduces hallucinated object mentions by up to 21.3% across multiple 7B backbones while preserving or improving performance, making it valuable for real-world deployment. ReWEIGH caches image evidence during prefill and adds only 1.33% average latency per token. It demonstrates effectiveness across six architecture families up to 32B parameters, and the method is training-free, requiring only unlabeled images for reference estimation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large vision-language models (LVLMs) extend large language models to process both images and text, but they often generate content not supported by the input image, known as hallucination. Existing mitigation methods often require training or fine-tuning, which is costly. ReWEIGH leverages the model's visual-token states and vocabulary ranks to provide a scale-invariant measure of visual evidence, enabling a training-free decoding intervention.

## [MDTIM: Masked Diffusion Model for Time Series Imputation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19119) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MDTIM, a masked diffusion model for time series imputation that separates missing and observed values using a structurally orthogonal MASK token and directly predicts original signals instead of added noise. It also proposes Stochastic Discretization to bridge discrete masked diffusion with continuous time series data. This approach addresses two key limitations in existing time series imputation methods, potentially improving accuracy and robustness across various missing data scenarios. It could benefit applications in healthcare, finance, and IoT where missing data is common, and advance the integration of diffusion models with structured prediction tasks. MDTIM employs a masked diffusion training paradigm where the MASK token is orthogonal to valid observations, and the model is trained to predict original values directly. Stochastic Discretization maps continuous values to ordinal-aware tokens while preserving continuous dynamics, and experiments show superior robustness and scalability over state-of-the-art baselines.

## [PGFS++ Improves Molecular Property Optimization with Synthesis Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19121) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PGFS++ introduces a synthesis-aware reinforcement learning framework for molecular property improvement, addressing reactant selection and reward-hacking issues. It builds on PGFS and PGFS+ to achieve better property improvement while preserving output diversity. This work addresses a critical challenge in AI-driven drug discovery: optimizing molecules that are not only effective but also synthesizable. By mitigating reward hacking and improving diversity, PGFS++ could enhance the practical utility of computational drug design pipelines. PGFS+ replaces reactant embedding prediction with trainable embedding lookup tables for reaction templates and second reactants, improving learning effectiveness. However, it exposes a reward-hacking failure mode where a powerful reactant search collapses output diversity; PGFS++ counters this by treating the input molecule as the start of a forward-synthesis trajectory and applying learned templates with in-stock building blocks.

## [Group-Calibrated On-Policy Distillation Boosts Long-Context Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19181) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Group-Calibrated On-Policy Distillation (GC-OPD), a method that calibrates token-level teacher guidance with response-level verifier rewards to improve long-context reasoning in LLMs. On five benchmarks, GC-OPD raises the average scores of Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-8B from 29.08 to 40.47 and from 35.12 to 44.65, respectively, outperforming vanilla OPD. This work addresses a critical mismatch in on-policy distillation for long-context tasks, where token-level teacher guidance can conflict with task-level verifier rewards. By effectively combining dense guidance with sparse rewards, GC-OPD offers a practical way to improve reasoning performance in smaller models, which is valuable for model compression and deployment. GC-OPD normalizes verifier rewards and trajectory-level OPD scores within each rollout group, using their difference as a signed disagreement residual. It then applies relative-advantage-based credit assignment (RACA) to distribute this residual across tokens while preserving the original OPD signal, and ablations show the signed residual and RACA each contribute to the gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student model on its own responses using dense token-level guidance from a stronger teacher, which is effective for many tasks but can be suboptimal for long-context reasoning where evidence is distributed across the input. Task-specific verifiers provide response-level rewards that better reflect task completion, but integrating them with token-level guidance is challenging. This paper proposes a group-calibrated approach to reconcile these two signals.

Researchers propose a hybrid method combining reinforcement learning (RL) with search-based path planners to accelerate flight trajectory optimization. The approach achieves up to 50% faster computation while keeping fuel consumption within 1% of the unconstrained solver, as validated using Airbus performance models. This innovation is crucial for emergency rerouting in aviation, where rapid path recalculation can be life-saving. By significantly reducing computation time without compromising fuel efficiency, it could enhance real-time decision-making and operational resilience for airlines. The RL agent pre-computes near-optimal paths based on location and atmospheric data, which are then used to constrain the search-based solver's search space. While global optimality is not guaranteed, empirical results show fuel deviations typically within 1% and speed improvements up to 50% compared to a conventional solver alone.

## [When to Distill Multi-Agent Workflows into Single-Agent Skills](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.01608) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Behavior-Outcome Freedom (F), a pre-synthesis diagnostic that predicts when distilling multi-agent workflows into single-agent skills is beneficial, and proposes AdaSkill, a two-stage adaptive distillation framework. Empirical results show that adding pipeline guidance can improve utility by +19.6 points under method-selection accuracy but decrease it by -10.3 points under numerical error, revealing a nuanced reversal. This research addresses a critical question in AI/ML about the trade-offs between multi-agent orchestration overhead and single-agent efficiency, offering a principled diagnostic to guide agent design. It could help practitioners decide when to simplify complex workflows, potentially reducing deployment costs and latency while maintaining performance. The study formalizes the reversal through Signed Anchor-Rank Transfer and shows that the Full-minus-Discard effect decreases across the continuous F scale (r = -0.80, p < 0.001) across 16 capability-matched interventions. AdaSkill is evaluated on 11 datasets spanning four structured data-science task families, achieving strong performance with up to 8 times lower cost and 15 times faster inference compared to full multi-agent systems.

**Background**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) for structured data-science tasks distribute expertise across stages, tools, shared state, verification, and repair, but incur coordination overhead and context fragmentation. Skill distillation compresses such workflows into a single-agent skill to reduce orchestration, but it is unclear which components should be transferred. The paper distinguishes capability resources (expanding what an agent can do) from pipeline guidance (constraining solution exploration), and introduces F to predict the trade-off.

This paper introduces a benchmark on the Speech Accessibility Project dataset, testing whether clinical context improves dysarthric speech recognition. Across nine models, they find that diagnosis-informed and clinically detailed prompts yield negligible or negative effects on word error rate, but LoRA fine-tuning with mixed clinical prompts achieves a 52% relative WER reduction. This work highlights a critical gap in current audio-language models: they fail to leverage multimodal clinical context for atypical speech, limiting accessibility. The study uses the Speech Accessibility Project dataset, which includes speech samples from people with diverse speech patterns. They compare nine models with prompts containing diagnosis labels, clinician ratings, and clinical descriptions, and also perform LoRA adaptation with mixed clinical prompt formats, achieving a WER of 0.066 (52% relative reduction) while preserving performance without context. ## [New Framework Measures Appropriate Reliance on Set-Valued AI Advice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06081) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the first formal framework for measuring appropriate reliance on set-valued AI advice, covering both classification and regression tasks. It defines new metrics such as correct reliance rate on AI and self for classification, and quantity and quality of AI reliance for regression. This framework fills a critical gap in human-AI collaboration research, as existing measures only consider point predictions. It enables more accurate evaluation of how humans use set-valued AI advice, which is increasingly common in real-world applications like medical diagnosis and price estimation, potentially improving AI system design and human decision-making. For classification, the framework introduces necessary dimensions for evaluating set-valued advice and defines two metrics: correct reliance rate on AI and correct reliance rate on self. For regression, it introduces quantity of AI reliance and quality of AI reliance, measuring whether the decision maker utilized the advice and whether it improved accuracy relative to their initial estimate.

ContextSniper, AntTrail's code-repair module, introduces precision evidence selection for repository-level program repair, reducing token usage by 51.5% and cost by 36.4% on SWE-bench Lite for OpenClaw, with similar gains for Claude Code. It indexes code and action memory into three abstract levels, uses a hybrid ranker, and filters long tool output via an intention-aware context gate. This matters because token and cost overhead are major barriers to scaling LLM agents for real-world repository repair. By cutting token use by roughly half without sacrificing repair quality, ContextSniper could make such agents more practical and affordable for broader adoption. In a matched 50-task-per-condition comparison on SWE-bench Lite, ContextSniper reduced total token use by 51.5% and logged cost by 36.4% for OpenClaw, and by 38.9% and 27.3% for Claude Code, with submitted-resolution rates essentially unchanged. A separate five-task comparison showed ContextSniper outperforming existing memory- and RAG-style integrations on token efficiency.

<a id="item-193"></a>
## [G-ReAct: Graph-Guided Deep Search via Structure-State Co-Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01324) ⭐️ 7.0/10

G-ReAct is a new reasoning framework for deep search that organizes reasoning as state evolution over a fixed-topology query graph, explicitly tracking search progress to mitigate context forgetting and search drift. It supports both supervised fine-tuning and inference-time guidance, and with only 1.9K generated trajectories, Qwen3-30B-A3B-Thinking-2507 achieves 52.6% accuracy on BrowseComp-ZH and 79.0% on XBench, outperforming comparable open-source methods. This framework addresses a known limitation in LLM deep search—linear sequential reasoning that causes context forgetting and search drift—by introducing graph-guided state evolution, which could improve multi-hop reasoning and search efficiency. It offers a lightweight training approach (only 1.9K trajectories) that outperforms larger datasets and RL-enhanced methods, potentially lowering the cost of building strong deep-search models. G-ReAct uses a fixed-topology query graph where the evolving graph state guides subsequent decisions, transforming exploratory search into graph-guided reasoning under explicit constraints. The framework is model-agnostic and can be applied at inference time without additional fine-tuning, consistently improving strong LLMs on deep-search tasks. The authors plan to release all code and model weights.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Deep search in LLMs involves multi-hop reasoning over open-domain tasks, where the model must retrieve and integrate information across multiple steps. Traditional approaches often rely on linear sequential reasoning, which struggles to maintain intermediate states and constraints over long horizons, leading to context forgetting and search drift. Graph-based reasoning methods, such as Think-on-Graph, have been explored to improve reasoning by structuring knowledge, but G-ReAct specifically focuses on state evolution over a query graph for deep search.
