<a id="item-195"></a>
## [AdmitOR: Label-Free Admission Gate for LLM Optimization Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces AdmitOR, a label-free admission gate for experience-learning agents in LLM-based optimization modeling, which uses calibrated external behavioral evidence to avoid unreliable knowledge admission. It achieves an admission precision of 0.927, significantly outperforming baseline methods. This work addresses a critical challenge in LLM-based optimization modeling: how to safely admit knowledge without ground-truth labels, which is common in real-world ticket streams. By reducing poisoned admissions by 3.1x to 8.0x compared to baselines, AdmitOR could improve the reliability and performance of experience-learning agents in practical applications. AdmitOR runs candidates from three model families, prompting strategies, and solver stacks on resampled instances, summarizing agreement via a cross-family clique and using a calibrated threshold to return accept, abstain, or escalate. The preregistered false-discovery criterion holds on calibration data but not on the wild stream, with failures traced to benchmark texts that do not faithfully encode their labeled instances.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Experience-learning agents for optimization modeling improve by storing verified skills, but existing learners admit knowledge by checking against known answers, which real ticket streams do not provide. Label-free alternatives like admitting every executable model or single-instance agreement are unreliable, poisoning roughly one in four admissions or accepting models that match at one value but differ elsewhere. AdmitOR is the first label-free admission mechanism designed around an explicitly calibrated false-discovery target.

## [Model Cascades Under Data Perturbations: Robustness and Pareto-Optimal Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17711) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates how data perturbations affect confidence-based routing in model cascades for image classification, identifying three failure modes and proposing a Pareto-optimal selection method that balances accuracy, routing quality, and energy efficiency, achieving up to a 10-fold reduction in CO2 emissions. This work highlights the importance of evaluating model cascades beyond clean accuracy, emphasizing routing reliability under distribution shift. It provides practical insights for building energy-efficient AI systems that remain robust in real-world scenarios, which is crucial for sustainable AI deployment. The study focuses on image classification and analyzes static corruptions and sequential perturbations. It identifies three failure modes: (1) routing signal breaks while the large model remains useful, (2) both models degrade so deferral no longer recovers accuracy, and (3) predictions stabilize but deferral suppresses, leading to stable but unreliable predictions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Model cascades are a technique to reduce AI energy consumption by routing easy inputs through a small model and deferring difficult cases to a larger model. Confidence-based routing relies on the model's confidence scores to decide whether to defer, but data perturbations can shift these scores, affecting routing decisions. Pareto-optimal selection involves choosing a cascade configuration that balances multiple objectives, such as accuracy, routing quality, and energy consumption.

## [Policy Counting Tames Exploding DecPOMDPs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17749) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces policy-counted DecPOMDPs, a new formulation that counts policies instead of agents, and presents a dynamic programming method to solve them efficiently, achieving tractability in the number of agents. This work addresses the exponential complexity of DecPOMDPs in multi-agent systems, which is a major bottleneck for real-world applications. By shifting from counting agents to counting policies, it opens new avenues for scalable multi-agent decision making under uncertainty. The paper provides three contributions: a new definition of counting DecPOMDPs that prevents policy space explosion, an updated utility calculation with tractability analysis, and a dynamic programming operator for solving policy-counted DecPOMDPs. The approach achieves polynomial dependence on the number of agents.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Decentralized Partially Observable Markov Decision Processes (DecPOMDPs) are a general framework for multi-agent decision making under uncertainty, but they suffer from exponential complexity in the number of agents. Previous approaches exploited symmetry among agents by counting agents, but this led to an explosion in the policy space. This paper redirects the focus to counting policies, which enables tractability.

## [D2ACCI: A Dual-Loop Diagnostic Protocol for Evidence-Preserving Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17756) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces D2ACCI, a dual-loop diagnostic protocol for localizing and controlling failures in LLM agent memory pipelines, along with a graded observability metric (DCR) and a reusable evaluation artifact (D2ACCI-Eval). The protocol was instantiated in MemStack and evaluated on three public benchmarks, achieving 93.59% on LoCoMo, 90.93% on LongMemEval, and 57.20% on PersonaMem-V2. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent memory evaluation by providing a systematic method to localize failures to specific pipeline stages, which is essential for reliable iteration and deployment of memory systems. The protocol's emphasis on paired statistical evidence and regression-aware checks could influence how the AI/ML community evaluates and improves agent memory. D2ACCI uses a dual-loop design with an outer diagnostic gate that promotes, feature-flags, or rejects memory interventions based on paired evidence, protected-slice monitoring, and trace-level localizability. The DCR metric measures whether failures remain localizable, and D2ACCI-Eval enables gate replay; five paired ablations showed statistically significant gains (+1.9 to +3.7pp, all p ≤ .003), while BM25/RRF was retained as a monitored feature flag.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents rely on memory to maintain context across sessions, but their multi-stage pipelines (ingestion, retrieval, filtering, generation) make it difficult to pinpoint where errors occur. Traditional end-to-end evaluation only shows that an error happened, not which stage caused it, and often lacks statistical rigor. D2ACCI addresses this by providing a diagnostic protocol that combines paired statistical tests, slice-level non-regression checks, and trace-level analysis to ensure evidence-based iteration.

## [CausalProfiler: Synthetic Benchmarks for Causal ML Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22842) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CausalProfiler is a new synthetic benchmark generator for causal machine learning (Causal ML) that randomly samples causal models, data, queries, and ground truths from user-defined spaces, providing coverage guarantees and transparent assumptions across observation, intervention, and counterfactual levels. This is the first such generator with these features, as presented in an arXiv paper (arXiv:2511.22842) and accepted as an ICML 2026 poster. Causal ML is crucial for high-stakes decisions, but existing benchmarks rely on limited hand-crafted datasets, leading to brittle conclusions. CausalProfiler enables rigorous, reproducible, and transparent evaluation under diverse conditions, helping researchers and practitioners validate methods more reliably and advance the field. The generator operates on the three levels of causal reasoning: observation, intervention, and counterfactual, and includes coverage guarantees to ensure sampled benchmarks cover the specified space. It was demonstrated by evaluating several state-of-the-art methods both in and out of the identification regime, showing the types of analyses it enables.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Causal machine learning combines prediction with causal inference to answer 'what-if' questions, going beyond correlation to understand cause-and-effect relationships. Synthetic benchmark generators create artificial datasets with known ground truths, allowing controlled evaluation of methods; CausalProfiler extends this to causal models and queries, addressing the scarcity of diverse benchmarks in Causal ML.

## [FiLoRA: Instruction-Conditioned LoRA for Controllable Feature Reliance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02060) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FiLoRA (Focus-and-Ignore LoRA) is introduced as an instruction-conditioned, parameter-efficient adaptation framework that enables explicit control over internal feature reliance in multimodal foundation models. It decomposes LoRA updates into feature-aligned low-rank modules and applies instruction-conditioned gating, allowing natural language instructions to modulate which features the model focuses on or ignores. This work addresses a critical gap in multimodal model interpretability and control, moving beyond post hoc analysis or output-level prompting to directly intervene on internal feature pathways. It offers a practical mechanism for improving robustness against spurious features and provides a new perspective on controllability in multimodal systems, which could benefit applications requiring fine-grained control over model behavior. FiLoRA keeps the task and predictive objective fixed while modulating feature reliance through instruction-conditioned gating. It is evaluated across controlled classification settings and generative multimodal tasks, under natural and compositional instructions, demonstrating consistent and interpretable shifts in feature reliance without altering task semantics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal foundation models integrate heterogeneous signals across modalities, but their reliance on specific internal features is often opaque. Existing approaches to shortcut and spurious behavior rely on post hoc analysis or data-level interventions, offering limited direct control. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adds small trainable low-rank matrices instead of updating all weights. FiLoRA extends LoRA by decomposing updates into feature-aligned modules and applying instruction-conditioned gating, enabling natural language to act as computation-level control signals.

## [FHCR Enables Whole-Piece Training of Symbolic Music Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.19816) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Full-Horizon Compressed Recurrence (FHCR), a framework that enables training symbolic music language models on complete compositions under limited GPU memory by compressing the key-value representation while preserving the full temporal horizon. It also proposes KV-Reset Context Utilization (KRCU), an evaluation-time diagnostic to measure long-range context utilization. This work addresses a key limitation of fixed-length training in symbolic music generation, potentially improving the coherence and musicality of generated pieces. It also offers a general approach for long-context modeling in resource-constrained settings, which could benefit other sequence modeling domains. The framework compresses the KV representation of recurrent memory to reduce memory cost while maintaining the full temporal horizon. Experiments on the MAESTRO dataset show that FHCR preserves long-range context utilization compared to models with reduced temporal memory, and KRCU reveals that full-horizon models utilize context far beyond the local segment window.

**Background**: Symbolic music language models are typically trained on fixed-length sequences for computational efficiency, but musical structure spans entire compositions. Recurrent memory can capture long-range dependencies, but its key-value representation grows with sequence length, causing memory issues. FHCR compresses this representation to enable whole-piece training, and KRCU provides a way to measure how well models use long-range context.

## [Wildfire Suppression Resource Allocation Proved NP-Complete; New MIP and Instance Generator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29865) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves strong NP-completeness for wildfire suppression resource allocation on planar graphs and full weighted directed grids, and introduces a new mixed-integer programming (MIP) formulation that achieves state-of-the-art results. It also presents a physics-grounded instance generator based on Rothermel's surface fire spread model. These theoretical and methodological contributions advance operations research for environmental management, providing both complexity boundaries and a competitive MIP approach that challenges earlier findings. The realistic instance generator enables more meaningful benchmarking, potentially improving wildfire response strategies. The paper proves strong NP-completeness for the problem and two variants on planar graphs, and for two of the three problems on full weighted directed grids, even when all resources are released simultaneously. The new MIP formulation outperforms existing methods, and the instance generator uses Rothermel's model to create diverse, realistic benchmarks.

**Background**: Wildfire suppression resource allocation involves deciding how to deploy limited firefighting resources over time to slow fire spread on a graph-based landscape. NP-completeness indicates that finding optimal solutions is computationally intractable for large instances, motivating heuristic or exact methods like MIP. Rothermel's surface fire spread model, developed in 1972, is a widely used semi-empirical model for predicting wildfire spread rate based on fuel, weather, and topography.

## [VLMs Violate Their Own Color-Labeling Rules, Humans Don't](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06422) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces the Graded Color Attribution (GCA) dataset, a controlled benchmark with line drawings varying pixel-level color coverage, and finds that VLMs like GPT-5-mini violate their stated introspective color-labeling rules in nearly 60% of cases on objects with strong color priors, while humans remain faithful to their rules. This challenges the assumption that VLM reasoning failures are difficulty-driven, suggesting instead that their introspective self-knowledge is miscalibrated. It has direct implications for trustworthy AI deployment in high-stakes applications where models must reliably predict and adhere to their own decision rules. The GCA dataset includes three conditions: world-knowledge recolorings, counterfactual recolorings, and shapes with no color priors. The study shows that VLMs can accurately estimate color coverage yet contradict their own reasoning, and that world-knowledge priors systematically degrade faithfulness across all models and strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding, but their reliability is often undermined by issues like object hallucination and overconfidence. Introspective reasoning refers to a model's ability to analyze, monitor, and revise its own reasoning processes, which is crucial for trustworthy deployment. The GCA dataset is designed to elicit explicit decision rules from models and humans, then test whether they follow those rules in practice.

## [MedStruct-S: New Benchmark for OCR Clinical Report Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03103) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MedStruct-S is a new benchmark containing 3,582 annotated real-world clinical report pages, designed to evaluate key discovery, key-conditioned QA, and end-to-end key-value extraction under unknown keys and OCR noise. It benchmarks four encoder-only and five decoder-only models ranging from 0.11B to 103B parameters. This benchmark addresses critical real-world challenges in clinical data processing, such as heterogeneous key representations and OCR-induced noise, which are often under-modeled in existing evaluations. It provides a reliable basis for model selection across different semi-structured information extraction settings, potentially improving the reconstruction of patients' longitudinal medical histories. The benchmark includes three tasks: field-header (key) discovery, key-conditioned QA, and end-to-end key-value pair extraction, with both exact match and answer match metrics. Results show that encoder-only models achieve the best performance for non-null-value key-conditioned QA despite being smaller, while fine-tuned decoder-only models deliver the strongest overall results when model scale is not controlled.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Semi-structured information extraction from OCR-derived clinical reports is crucial for reconstructing patients' longitudinal medical histories, especially when reports are fragmented across institutions due to privacy regulations. Existing evaluations often overlook heterogeneous and incompletely known key representations and OCR-induced noise, making it difficult to assess model robustness in real-world settings. MedStruct-S aims to fill this gap by providing a standardized benchmark with realistic conditions.

## [DELOS: Contrastive Deep Learning Boosts Low-SNR Transit Detection in Kepler Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29428) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DELOS, a deep-learning framework using contrastive scoring, performs blind searches for shallow transits in Kepler photometry without relying on pre-detected events. It improves precision-recall by 15.5% over BLS and 11.25% over TLS in the low-SNR regime, while accelerating searches by factors of 3-5 and 74-80, respectively. This work provides an efficient and sensitive method for detecting shallow transits, which is crucial for finding longer-period terrestrial planets. It represents a practical step toward future searches in missions like TESS, PLATO, and Earth 2.0, potentially expanding our knowledge of exoplanets. DELOS was trained on 20 million synthetic light curves with realistic transit models and Kepler-like noise, achieving 99.3% validation accuracy. It uses GPU-accelerated phase folding, optimized phase binning, and a custom 1D convolutional encoder to produce a score periodogram over trial periods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Transit searches in photometric data aim to detect periodic dips in brightness caused by planets passing in front of their host stars. Traditional methods like Box-fitting Least Squares (BLS) and Transit Least Squares (TLS) are widely used but struggle with low signal-to-noise ratios. Contrastive learning is a deep learning technique that learns representations by comparing similar and dissimilar samples, which DELOS leverages to assign transit-likeness scores.

## [Hybrid ANN-SNN Pipeline Achieves 99.09% Accuracy on ImageNet](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20151) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a hybrid ANN-SNN pipeline that combines a pretrained EfficientNet encoder with a CoLaNET spiking classifier, trained via local plasticity rules. The system achieves 99.09% accuracy on a 64-class ImageNet benchmark without end-to-end gradient propagation. This work demonstrates that biologically plausible local learning rules can achieve performance comparable to conventional deep networks on a large-scale benchmark, potentially enabling more energy-efficient neuromorphic hardware. It bridges the gap between powerful pretrained ANNs and efficient SNNs, which could accelerate adoption of SNNs in real-world applications. The encoder's activations are converted into spike trains via rate-coding, and the SNN classifier is trained using local, biologically inspired learning rules, bypassing backpropagation. The reported 99.09% accuracy is on a 64-class subset of ImageNet, not the full 1000-class dataset, which is a notable limitation.

**Background**: Spiking neural networks (SNNs) are brain-inspired models that process information as discrete spikes, offering potential energy efficiency on neuromorphic hardware. However, training SNNs is challenging due to their non-differentiable nature; local plasticity rules like STDP provide biologically plausible alternatives to backpropagation. Hybrid ANN-SNN pipelines leverage pretrained ANNs to extract features while using SNNs for classification, aiming to combine the best of both paradigms.

## [Mask2Real-WM: Segmentation Masks Bridge Sim-to-Real for Dexterous World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04546) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Mask2Real-WM introduces a two-stage action-conditioned world model for dexterous manipulation that decouples dynamics and rendering, predicting future segmentation masks and rendering them to photorealistic RGB using a ControlNet-augmented Stable Video Diffusion backbone. The dynamics model is pretrained on over 50 hours of synthetic data and fine-tuned on fewer than 2.5 hours of real demonstrations, achieving per-DoF action controllability across all 23 degrees of freedom. This work addresses the sim-to-real gap in dexterous manipulation by leveraging the smaller gap in segmentation space, enabling efficient transfer from simulation to real robots. It demonstrates that mask conditioning and simulation pretraining are both essential for fine-grained per-joint control, which could improve data efficiency and policy learning in robot learning. The dynamics model predicts future segmentation masks from past masks and 23-DoF action sequences, while the rendering model uses a ControlNet-augmented Stable Video Diffusion backbone to map masks to photorealistic RGB. Experiments on a dexterous pick-and-place benchmark show that monolithic baselines capture broad trajectories but fail to reflect fine-grained per-joint action effects, highlighting the need for the two-stage decoupling.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Action-conditioned world models allow robots to predict future consequences of candidate actions without physical interaction, supporting policy evaluation, planning, and data augmentation. Sim-to-real transfer is a common challenge in robot learning, where models trained in simulation often fail when deployed on real hardware due to the reality gap. Segmentation masks provide a more abstract representation that is less sensitive to visual domain differences, making them a promising bridge for sim-to-real transfer.

This work demonstrates that LLMs can serve as effective AutoML agents for neural architecture search, potentially reducing the need for manual design and enabling scalable, script-adaptive OCR across languages. It could accelerate deployment of handwriting recognition in diverse linguistic contexts. The framework requires no manual architecture design, domain-specific preprocessing, or hyperparameter tuning, and achieves inference latency between 41 and 44 milliseconds. ## [RouteCost: Multi-Stage Framework for E-Commerce Shipping Cost Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16230) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RouteCost, a production-inspired multi-stage framework for pre-order shipping cost estimation in e-commerce, was introduced in an arXiv preprint. It decomposes the problem into time-aware demand forecasting, fee-card-informed baseline pricing, residual correction, and proxy-based box-consolidation inference, and was validated on over 250,000 orders, 260 products, and 18 months of order history. Accurate shipping cost estimation is critical for e-commerce price presentation, margin planning, and conversion rates. RouteCost's multi-stage approach could improve predictive quality and calibration over static lookup methods and monolithic regressors, potentially benefiting e-commerce platforms and sellers by reducing cost estimation errors and enhancing operational efficiency. The framework uses a route-weighted expectation formulation to aggregate route-level cost estimates into product-level predictions. It addresses factors such as billable weight, dimensional pricing, surcharge triggers, and latent operational effects like shipment consolidation, while preserving route-level interpretability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: In e-commerce, shipping costs are influenced by distance, destination demand mix, billable weight (the greater of actual and dimensional weight), dimensional pricing (charging based on package volume), surcharge triggers, and operational factors like shipment consolidation. Traditional static lookup methods fail to capture these variations, while monolithic regressors may exploit non-causal correlations. RouteCost decomposes the problem into multiple stages to address these complexities.

## [M2Patch: Structured Latent Space for Multivariate Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19404) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces M2Patch, a CNN-based architecture that uses multi-scale temporal patching and two differentiable penalties (intra-scale smoothness and inter-scale alignment) to enforce structural consistency in the latent space for multivariate time series forecasting. Experiments on ten real-world benchmarks show it significantly outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. This work addresses a key limitation in existing patching and multi-scale methods, which treat learned representations as transient byproducts rather than enforcing structural consistency. By introducing explicit penalties, M2Patch improves forecasting accuracy and provides a structure-aware representation that can recover channel functional groupings, potentially benefiting applications like traffic and weather forecasting. M2Patch uses depthwise separable CNN blocks with progressively growing dilation to extract scale-specific features at linear complexity, and per-scale learned projections compress features into a compact latent representation. The intra-scale smoothness penalty enforces temporal continuity between adjacent patches, while the inter-scale alignment penalty restores cross-granularity interaction through learnable cross-scale mappings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Multivariate time series forecasting is crucial in fields like traffic management and weather prediction, where data exhibit complex temporal variations. Recent methods use patching and multi-scale approaches to capture temporal correlations, but they often lack explicit mechanisms to ensure structural consistency across scales. M2Patch builds on these ideas by introducing a structured latent space with penalties that enforce smoothness and alignment, improving both accuracy and interpretability.

## [New Metric Exposes Dependency Gap in Tabular Generative Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21636) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a new evaluation metric for tabular generative models that decomposes classification-based scores into marginal, dependency, and cross terms, revealing a persistent dependency gap in flow-matching and diffusion models. The metric shows that current metrics like linear C2ST are blind to inter-column dependencies. This work addresses a critical blind spot in evaluating synthetic tabular data, as inter-column dependencies are essential for data utility. The proposed metric provides a more accurate diagnostic tool, potentially improving the development and certification of generative models for tabular data. The metric uses a gradient-boosted C2ST and decomposes its score against zero-dependency and real-data references. Experiments show that destroying dependencies collapses minority-class F1 by 0.38-0.61, and the dependency gap persists even with 16x capacity increase, but shrinks with 8x capacity reduction.

**Background**: Tabular generative models aim to create synthetic data that preserves both column-wise distributions and inter-column dependencies. The classifier two-sample test (C2ST) is a common evaluation method, but linear classifiers often fail to detect dependency violations. Gradient boosting is a powerful machine learning technique that can capture complex relationships in tabular data.

## [Untrainable Elements Break Scale Invariance in Physical Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00097) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper separates the effects of scale invariance and mass conservation in physical learning rules, showing that untrainable elements break homogeneity and cause the learned function to depend on initialization scale. This finding clarifies the inductive biases in physical learning, which is crucial for designing neuromorphic and resistive network devices where untrainable elements are common. It could guide the development of more robust physical learning algorithms. Across twenty topologies, the learned function shifts with initialization scale by a median of 12% with fixed rectifiers and 8% with fixed linear resistors, versus 3e-8 when all elements are trainable. A single fixed rectifier produces the entire effect.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Physical learning rules like equilibrium propagation (EP), coupled learning (CL), and adjoint coupled learning (AL) train resistive networks using local measurements. These rules rely on the circuit's physical dynamics to compute gradients, and the learned function is determined by where training lands on the solution manifold.

## [AI Anthropomorphism as Institutional Bias: A Critical Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00961) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper critiques the dominant framing of AI anthropomorphism as user error, arguing it reflects institutional bias and imposes disproportionate harms on neurodivergent users. It challenges the epistemic authority of institutions that pathologize relational AI interactions without adequate justification. This work is significant because it reframes a common AI ethics issue, highlighting how power dynamics and institutional norms shape our understanding of human-AI interaction. It could influence future AI design and policy to be more inclusive of neurodivergent users and those in crisis. The paper does not argue whether anthropomorphic interpretations are correct, but questions whether governing bodies have met the conditions to determine them. It outlines methodological commitments for an equitable framing, emphasizing the self-validating evidentiary loop that reproduces the dominant narrative.

## [Epistemic Transfer Framework for AI-Assisted Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.08882) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a framework and evaluation protocol for measuring the lasting impact of AI-assisted verification on users' unassisted performance, defining the Epistemic Transfer Effect (ETE) and Tool-Removal Cost (TRC). This work addresses a gap in current evaluation methods by focusing on what users retain after AI tool removal, which is crucial for understanding the long-term value and potential de-skilling effects of AI assistance. It provides a diagnostic space that can inform the design and deployment of AI tools in contexts where independent judgment is critical. The protocol combines answer-first and evidence-first AI conditions with active-practice and no-practice controls, delayed tests on held-out claims, and behavioral measures. ETE compares delayed unassisted performance across conditions, while TRC measures the immediate drop when the tool is removed, together yielding a diagnostic space for capability building, tool advantage, epistemic inertness, and verification on loan.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: AI tools that assist users in judging online claims are typically evaluated while the tool is present, but this paper asks what users can do on their own afterward. The concept of epistemic transfer is distinct from related outcomes like correction effects, trust, reliance, and human-AI team performance. The paper proposes a practical evaluation protocol that can be used in online experiments or field studies, emphasizing the importance of testing not only immediate help but also the lasting impact on independent judgment.

## [BrainWAM Coordinates Semantic Priors and Predictive Dynamics for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12854) ⭐️ 7.0/10

BrainWAM, a novel framework, integrates semantic priors from Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models and predictive dynamics from World Action Models (WAMs) via a structured action-space coordination mechanism, achieving state-of-the-art performance on NAVSIM v1 (89.5 PDMS) and NAVSIM v2 (89.6 EPDMS). It also introduces an asynchronous rectified-flow inference strategy to reduce latency. This work addresses a critical gap in autonomous driving by unifying semantic reasoning and predictive dynamics, which are often treated separately. The proposed coordination framework could lead to more robust and human-like driving behavior, potentially improving safety and efficiency in real-world applications. BrainWAM converts semantic reasoning and predictive world modeling into two specialized action-oriented pathways and aligns them at the level of compact action representations, avoiding the attention-allocation mismatch found in naive token-level attention fusion. The asynchronous rectified-flow inference decouples video and action denoising, shortening inference latency while preserving planning-relevant predictive context.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous driving planning requires both semantic understanding (e.g., traffic rules, scene semantics) and predictive dynamics (e.g., future motion of other agents). VLA models leverage large vision-language models for semantic reasoning, while WAMs focus on generative world modeling for future prediction. However, naive fusion of these two approaches often leads to attention-allocation mismatch, where semantic shortcuts dominate and suppress predictive dynamics. BrainWAM draws inspiration from neuroscience, where complex behavior arises from coordination among functionally specialized systems.

<a id="item-221"></a>
## [Cross-Model Memory Transfer via Target-Side Reader Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17050) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper investigates whether the frozen hashed memory or the target-side reader is more critical when transferring learned memory across different LLM backbones. It introduces cross-model frozen-memory extraction, where a memory trained on a source model is frozen and attached to a different target model, with only a lightweight reader trained. This work addresses a timely question about efficient knowledge integration in LLMs, potentially enabling reusable external knowledge artifacts that can be transferred across models. It could reduce the cost of adapting knowledge to new backbones and improve flexibility in model deployment. The study uses a dual-layer, four-branch reader that nearly closes the gap between same-model and cross-model reuse, achieving an average score of 38.8 under a controlled evaluation protocol. When the provider reader is directly compatible with the target interface, the frozen artifact provides substantial utility without target-side training, while optional reader adaptation yields further improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models typically store knowledge either through non-parametric retrieval (external, flexible but with latency and shallow integration) or parametric adaptation (efficient but entangled with weights). Engram-style hashed memory offers a middle ground: it stores learned information in an external, addressable table and consumes it via a small learned reader. This paper explores how such memory can be transferred across different backbones, focusing on the roles of the frozen memory and the target-side reader.

## [Metamorphic Artificial Age Score for Drone Propeller Health Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18088) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a Metamorphic Artificial Age Score (AAS) decision-support prototype for flight-log-based drone propeller health monitoring. Using the 2024 DronePropA dataset, it computes six health-related indicators and evaluates them with metamorphic adequacy relations and a redundancy-adjusted AAS formulation. This work addresses the challenge of detecting drone propeller faults that manifest across multiple flight-log channels rather than as a single signal. It supports post-flight maintenance prioritization and autonomous-system oversight, potentially improving drone safety and reliability in real-world operations. The framework computes six indicators: trajectory tracking error, attitude instability, thrust-command burden, motor-command imbalance, ESC-command instability, and battery-level stress. A retrospective evaluation with one healthy and three defective propeller cases showed different fault severities triggered different indicators, leading to maintenance review or mandatory inspection.

**Background**: Drone propeller faults can create safety risks, but their effects may be distributed across multiple flight-log channels. The DronePropA dataset provides motion trajectories and flight logs from 130 indoor flights with defective propellers, enabling research on health monitoring. Metamorphic testing is a technique that uses relations between inputs and outputs to verify software or systems without a ground truth.

## [Optimized Fuzzy Logic with IEEE Key Gas Method Boosts Transformer Fault Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18133) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces FL-KGM, an optimized fuzzy logic approach combined with the IEEE Key Gas Method, achieving up to 98.6% accuracy in power transformer fault diagnosis. It refines membership functions, optimizes fuzzy rule sets, and separates CO and CO2 to eliminate diagnostic inconsistencies. Accurate transformer fault diagnosis is critical for power system stability, and FL-KGM significantly outperforms traditional KGM and other fuzzy logic methods. This advancement could enhance predictive maintenance and intelligent fault detection in modern power systems, reducing downtime and costs. The method leverages multidimensional gas ratio analysis and an adaptive classification framework, validated on real-world datasets. The separation of CO and CO2 is a novel step to address diagnostic inconsistencies in the IEEE Key Gas Method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Dissolved Gas Analysis (DGA) is a widely used technique for diagnosing faults in oil-immersed power transformers by analyzing gases dissolved in insulating oil. The IEEE Key Gas Method interprets key gas concentrations and ratios, but it struggles with ambiguous data. Fuzzy logic, introduced by Lotfi Zadeh, handles uncertainty and imprecision, making it suitable for improving DGA-based fault diagnosis.

## [GenEx: Graph-Based SARS-CoV-2 Variant Detection via Codon Co-occurrence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18238) ⭐️ 6.0/10

GenEx introduces a novel pipeline that converts raw SARS-CoV-2 gene sequences into codon co-occurrence graphs, extracting over 25 graph features using two techniques: MSCG and LAPCG. It also employs spectral graph feature extraction with SVD, using squared singular values to enhance inter-class separability, and trains 23 ML models to detect variants like Beta, Gamma, Delta, and Omicron. This graph-based paradigm moves beyond traditional sequence alignment methods, capturing contextual interdependencies in genomic data, which could improve variant detection accuracy and speed. It offers a novel cross-disciplinary approach (borrowing from computational linguistics) that may influence future bioinformatics methodologies for viral surveillance. The two key techniques are MSCG (Multi-Scale Codon Co-occurrence Graph) and LAPCG (Linear-time Adjacency PMI Codon Graph), which model codon sequences as structured symbolic vocabularies. The use of squared singular values (σ²) instead of eigenvalues in SVD amplifies separation between dominant and subdominant spectral components, enhancing classification performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional bioinformatics methods for viral variant detection, such as sequence alignment and phylogenetic analysis, treat gene sequences as linear strings, missing complex contextual dependencies. Co-occurrence networks, originally used in text analysis, represent relationships between entities; applying this concept to codons allows capturing these dependencies. GenEx leverages this idea, along with graph feature extraction and machine learning, to classify SARS-CoV-2 variants.

## [Candidate-Fate Accounting Enhances Transparency in Sensor Diagnostic Pipeline Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18665) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces candidate-fate accounting, a candidate-level audit framework for sensor diagnostic pipeline search that records every observed candidate, including invalid, pruned, skipped, cached, or unfitted ones, and assigns a terminal fate to each. Experiments on three bearing-diagnostic datasets show it detects invalid candidates and identifies 30–41 candidates omitted by fitted-trial-only reports. This framework addresses a critical gap in AutoML/AutoDL transparency by ensuring that all candidate pipelines are auditable, which is essential for reproducibility and reviewer trust. It could influence how automated machine learning systems report their search processes, benefiting fields that rely on sensor diagnostics and beyond. The framework uses hashes to merge repeated observations, legality checks to flag invalid candidates, allocation rationales to explain budget decisions, and a closed fate ledger to assign one terminal fate per candidate. The code is available on GitHub, and the experiments maintain competitive diagnostic performance while providing complete candidate accounting.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Automated machine learning (AutoML) and automated deep learning (AutoDL) systems search over pipelines of preprocessing, representation, and classification steps to reduce manual design cost. However, typical reports only retain fitted trials, scores, and winners, omitting many generated candidates, which limits transparency and reproducibility. Candidate-fate accounting closes this gap by recording every candidate's fate, enabling thorough auditing of the search process.

## [Multi-Agent Platform Boosts Enterprise Analytics Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18740) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new multi-agent platform built on CrewAI for conversational business intelligence achieves 95.3% functional accuracy and a 4.52/5.0 quality score, improving accuracy by 22.6 percentage points over a single-agent baseline. The system uses five specialized agents and the Model Context Protocol (MCP) for data retrieval and visualization. This work demonstrates a practical application of multi-agent systems for enterprise analytics, showing significant gains over single-agent approaches. It highlights the potential of combining frameworks like CrewAI and MCP to deliver reliable, secure, and reusable business intelligence solutions, which could influence how enterprises adopt AI for data-driven decision-making. The platform features a defense-in-depth security architecture for multi-tenant data isolation and a query parameterization mechanism for reusable dashboard components. Evaluation across 300 test cases showed a mean response latency of 24 seconds and a 93.0% hallucination-free rate, with an ablation study identifying Data Analysis and Report Aggregation agents as primary quality drivers.

LLM-as-a-Judge is a technique where a large language model evaluates the quality of another model's outputs, used here to assess response quality.

## [CES Algorithm Simplifies OWL Class Expressions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18899) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces CES (Class Expression Simplifier), a novel algorithm for syntactic simplification of OWL class expressions in Description Logics. CES applies rewriting rules to reduce complexity while preserving semantics, and is implemented in the open-source Python framework OWLAPY. This work improves reasoning efficiency and readability of OWL class expressions, which is crucial for ontology engineering, knowledge graph construction, and semantic web applications. By making ontologies more maintainable and scalable, it supports broader adoption of ontology-driven systems. CES was evaluated on class expressions learned from two medium-sized ontologies, showing measurable improvements in reasoning efficiency and verbosity reduction. The algorithm is publicly available as part of OWLAPY, an open-source Python framework for OWL.

StocksTalk is a new voice-enabled conversational system that converts spoken financial screening requests into validated SQL queries. It combines streaming speech recognition, retrieval-augmented constraint extraction, schema-grounded LLM-based SQL generation, rule-based validation, and human-in-the-loop verification, and includes a benchmark of 150 spoken financial prompts. This work demonstrates a transparent, voice-driven interface for financial analysis, potentially improving accessibility and trust in AI-assisted decision support. It addresses limitations of traditional template-driven assistants by exposing intermediate reasoning artifacts, which could influence future conversational AI systems in finance and other structured domains. The system integrates multiple components: streaming ASR, RAG for constraint extraction, schema-grounded SQL generation, rule-based validation, and human-in-the-loop verification. Experimental results show improvements in constraint extraction accuracy, SQL executability, logical consistency, and multi-turn stability compared to baseline LLM approaches.

## [One-Stage Wildlife Re-ID Model Combines DINOv2 and MegaDescriptor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18246) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a one-stage end-to-end detection and re-identification model for wildlife, integrating DINOv2 for spatial features and MegaDescriptor for re-identification, with prompt-based latent queries. It achieves a mean average precision of 30.584%, compared to 44.89% for a two-stage approach. This work explores a more efficient one-stage alternative to the traditional two-stage pipeline for wildlife re-identification, potentially enabling faster and simpler deployment in real-world monitoring. However, the accuracy gap highlights the trade-off between speed and performance, which is a key consideration for conservation applications. The model uses DINOv2 for robust spatial geometry and MegaDescriptor for wildlife re-identification, enhancing latent queries with prompt re-identification features. The reported mAP of 30.584% is significantly lower than the two-stage state-of-the-art (44.89%), and the paper is a preprint with preliminary results.

**Background**: Wildlife re-identification typically involves a two-stage pipeline: first detect animals, then match identities using re-identification models. One-stage detectors combine these steps into a single model, offering speed but often at the cost of accuracy. DINOv2 is a self-supervised vision foundation model from Meta AI that produces robust visual features, while MegaDescriptor is a specialized model for wildlife re-identification.

## [OptiModNet: Lightweight UNet-Transformer Hybrid for Optic Disc and Cup Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18516) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces OptiModNet, a lightweight hybrid architecture combining UNet and Transformer with grouped-query and channel attention, achieving state-of-the-art performance on the REFUGE2 dataset with 3.73 GFLOPs and 1.93M parameters, surpassing existing methods by over 2.5%. This work addresses the trade-off between accuracy and computational efficiency in glaucoma screening, enabling rapid, large-scale deployment in resource-limited clinical settings. It also demonstrates the potential of hybrid architectures in medical image segmentation, potentially influencing future model designs. OptiModNet integrates diverse attention mechanisms at multiple stages and uses an Aggregated Pyramid Loss to supervise predictions at multiple decoder depths, improving gradient flow and structural consistency. The model is evaluated on the REFUGE2 dataset for both optic disc and cup segmentation, and the code is publicly available on GitHub.

Hybrid architectures like UNetR combine both but add complexity. OptiModNet aims to balance these trade-offs with a lightweight design.

## [MorphoGP: Nonparametric Gaussian Process Framework for Predicting Tidal Beach Profiles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18558) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MorphoGP introduces a unified category-specific Gaussian process framework that uses contrastive learning to classify tide-influenced beach morphologies and then employs specialized Gaussian process experts to predict equilibrium beach profiles. Evaluated on over 180 beach profiles from the Chinese coast, it reduces test RMSE by about 59.3% compared to the best baseline, achieving a final RMSE of 0.297 m. This work addresses a critical challenge in coastal engineering: accurately predicting beach profiles under tidal influence, which is essential for sustainable coastal development and ecosystem management. By improving adaptability across diverse coastal environments, MorphoGP offers a more reliable data-driven tool that could enhance shoreline protection strategies and inform coastal management decisions. The framework consists of three components: a ContourCluster model for automatic classification, specialized Gaussian process experts for each morphological category, and a Gating Net that probabilistically integrates expert outputs. The study focuses on tide-influenced coasts along the Chinese coast, and the authors note that stronger process-level physical coupling remains an important direction for future development.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Equilibrium beach profiles (EBPs) describe the shape of a beach that is in dynamic equilibrium with wave, tide, and sediment conditions, and are widely used in coastal engineering projects. Traditional empirical and numerical models often struggle to adapt across diverse coastal environments, especially where tidal processes are significant. Gaussian processes are a nonparametric Bayesian method that provides uncertainty estimates and works well with small datasets, making them suitable for environmental modeling. Contrastive learning is an unsupervised technique that learns representations by contrasting positive and negative samples, which helps in classifying beach morphologies without extensive labeled data.

A new arXiv paper argues that AI hallucinations hold philosophical significance for machine consciousness, presenting empirical studies on GPT models under different temperature settings and an encoder-only model trained on encyclopedic data. This work challenges the view of hallucinations as mere engineering flaws, suggesting they may reveal insights into machine consciousness and the limits of AI self-reports. It could influence how researchers interpret AI behavior and the philosophical debate on machine sentience. The paper identifies causes like source-target divergence, training-inference discrepancies, and overfitting. It finds that higher temperatures yield plausible but incorrect answers, while lower temperatures yield accurate ones, and that hallucination rates correlate with parameters that make models seem creative.

## [LSADA: Learning-State-Aware Dynamic Generative Data Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.18907) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces LSADA, a novel generative data augmentation method that adapts augmentation strength per sample based on its learning state (loss and loss-decrease rate) and uses a decoupled diffusion fusion strategy to enhance diversity while preserving class semantics. Experiments on nine datasets show LSADA outperforms the existing SOTA dynamic GDA method by 4.5% on natural images and 2.5% on medical images. This work addresses a critical limitation in small-scale image classification by making data augmentation adaptive to the model's learning progress, which can significantly improve performance in data-scarce domains like medical imaging. It also advances the field of generative data augmentation by introducing a more nuanced, sample- and region-aware approach that balances diversity and semantic preservation. LSADA constructs a learning state for each sample using its current loss and loss-decrease rate, mapping it to a sample-specific augmentation strength. It also introduces a decoupled data augmentation and diffusion fusion strategy that applies strength-controlled transformations to class-relevant regions and generates diverse class-irrelevant regions, progressively fusing them. The method was evaluated on nine public datasets, achieving average improvements of 4.5% on six natural image datasets and 2.5% on three medical image datasets over the existing SOTA dynamic GDA method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: Generative data augmentation (GDA) uses pretrained generative models to create synthetic training data, which is especially useful when real data is scarce. Traditional GDA methods apply task-agnostic augmentation, ignoring the downstream model's needs, while recent dynamic methods incorporate model feedback but struggle with sample-specific strengths and region adaptation. LSADA builds on these ideas by using the model's learning state to guide augmentation and decoupling the augmentation of class-relevant and class-irrelevant regions to balance diversity and semantic consistency.

## [Sleeping Beauty Betting Strategies Coincide; Thirder Avoids Dutch Books](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.15911) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proves that Sleeping Beauty's ex ante and de se optimal betting strategies are identical, and that under asymptotically optimal betting, the Thirder position is invulnerable to diachronic Dutch Books while the Halfer position remains vulnerable. This result strengthens the case for the Thirder position in the Sleeping Beauty problem by linking it to Kelly betting and Dutch Book arguments, which are central to decision theory and probability. It may influence how philosophers and mathematicians evaluate rationality under imperfect recall. The paper uses the Kelly Criterion for asymptotically optimal betting, showing that a Thirder maximizes expected wealth growth rate. The analysis distinguishes between ex ante and de se perspectives, which are often conflated in previous discussions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: The Sleeping Beauty problem is a puzzle in decision theory where an agent is awakened once or twice based on a coin toss, with memory erased between awakenings, and asked her credence that the coin landed heads. The two main positions are 'Halfer' (probability 1/2) and 'Thirder' (probability 1/3). Dutch Book arguments show that certain belief sets lead to guaranteed losses, and diachronic Dutch Books extend this to changes in beliefs over time.

## [EEG Foundation Models Fail to Decode Long-Range Temporal Correlations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24834) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study evaluated five EEG foundation models (REVE, LaBraM, BENDR, CBraMod, BIOT) for decoding long-range temporal correlations measured by the detrended-fluctuation-analysis (DFA) exponent of the alpha-band amplitude envelope. Results showed that only BIOT and CBraMod showed some decoding in one cohort (CAUEEG), but these findings did not replicate in a second cohort (BrainLat), where all models performed at or below chance. This study provides critical evidence that current EEG foundation models may not capture long-range temporal dynamics, a key feature of neural activity, limiting their utility in neuroscience research and clinical applications. The lack of cross-cohort replication highlights the need for more robust evaluation and model development in this emerging field. The study used a common 240-second estimator with 8-13 Hz filtering, DFA over 2-23.8 seconds, artifact masking, and quality control. Notably, CBraMod and BIOT successfully decoded the aperiodic exponent in both cohorts (R-squared = 0.459-0.757), showing a spectral-temporal dissociation, but the DFA decoding was cohort-dependent and did not pass permutation testing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: EEG foundation models are large-scale machine learning architectures that learn universal representations from heterogeneous EEG data using self-supervised methods. Detrended fluctuation analysis (DFA) is a technique used to quantify fractal-like scaling properties of signals, often applied to EEG to measure long-range temporal correlations. The alpha-band amplitude envelope refers to the smooth curve outlining the instantaneous amplitude of alpha oscillations (8-13 Hz), which carries biologically relevant information beyond conventional amplitude and frequency measures.

<a id="item-249"></a>
## [Low-Rank Latent Carriers Enable Counterfactual Rollouts in World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15156) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper demonstrates that a rank-4 patch in the hidden state of a recurrent world model can redirect a 12-step autonomous rollout to follow a counterfactual trajectory, without future observations or teacher forcing. This is achieved by learning a map from factual state and requested edit to carrier coefficients. This work provides a compact intervention interface for steering learned world models, which could improve interpretability and control in model-based reinforcement learning. It suggests that low-rank latent edits can effectively guide predictions, potentially enabling more efficient counterfactual reasoning in complex environments. The study uses a 192-dimensional hidden state in a two-object, two-dimensional collision environment. Rank 4 is the smallest tested rank that satisfies the full development-panel criteria, and the frozen procedure passes preregistered replication across independently trained checkpoints. A position-edit stress test shows that successful editing alone is not sufficient, highlighting the need for dynamics-effective interventions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 20, 04:00

**Background**: World models are neural networks that learn to predict future states in an environment, often used in reinforcement learning for planning. Counterfactual rollouts involve simulating alternative futures by modifying the model's inputs or hidden states. Low-rank methods approximate high-dimensional changes with fewer parameters, making interventions more tractable.

## [Yandex Deep Tech Night Conference to Explore AI's Impact on Development](https://habr.com/ru/news/1069978/) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Yandex announced its Deep Tech Night conference, scheduled for September 5, focusing on how AI is transforming development, infrastructure, and data processes. The event will feature a keynote by Mo Gawdat, former Chief Business Officer at Google X, on the future beyond LLMs. This conference highlights the growing importance of AI in reshaping engineering roles and workflows, offering insights from a prominent AI thought leader. It is significant for developers and tech professionals seeking to understand the next wave of AI-driven changes in their field. The conference will be held online with a Q&A session, and recordings will be available only to registered participants. Other speakers include Pavel Kaplya, head of Alice's product development, discussing agentic products, and Andrey Popov, head of Machine Intelligence, sharing data on AI and productivity.

telegram · ai_newz · Aug 20, 17:03

**Background**: Yandex is a major Russian technology company known for its search engine and AI products like the voice assistant Alice. Mo Gawdat is an Egyptian author and former executive at Google X, where he served as Chief Business Officer; he is known for his books on happiness and AI, including 'Scary Smart'. The conference aims to address the practical challenges and opportunities AI presents for software development and infrastructure.
